### A fixed card-type order, and a Tokens list outside the 100 (DECK-TOKENS, 2026-10-24, the owner's ask; `DECK-TOKENS-session-prompt.md`)

- **The groups read in one fixed order, Magic's `display.typeOrder`**: the commander's zone, then Creatures, Sorceries, Instants, Artifacts, Enchantments, then the two the ask left out (Planeswalkers, Battles) ahead of Lands, then anything rarer by name and typeless cards last (`src/lib/games/type-order.ts`'s rank, shared with the adapter). Before, the groups sorted biggest first, so Lands led most decks and the sections swapped places while a deck was built. The editor, the share page and the "Card types" chart read the same constant, in text and grid and under every sort. One Piece declares none and keeps biggest first (LATER row 320). **Decision:** display only. Which group a card is in stays ingest's `primary_type`, so an artifact land is an Artifact in the list and in the Lands stat alike (row 321).
- **The Tokens section** is the format's `holds: "tokens"` zone (`countsTowardSize: false`), rendered after the card groups as one more group whose heading is the disclosure: Base UI's Collapsible trigger inside an h3 like the groups' h3s, the label beside its count, a chevron that turns, `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls` from the primitive. **Decision:** closed until opened, everywhere. The editor remembers the choice with the view preferences (`tokensOpen` in `deckwarden:deck-view`); the share page never stores it (its toggles read nothing during render). The editor's section waits for the first card of the 100, like View / Group / Sort (Y2a's quiet first screen), unless the deck already holds a token; the share page shows it only when the deck holds one.
- **The rows follow the pane's View.** A text row is the main list's row (quantity, steppers and remove in the editor) with the token's caption beside the name: `display.tokenCaption`, Magic's "1/1 white, flying", because token names don't tell tokens apart (fifteen Soldiers, thirty-one Elementals). **Decision:** commas, not a middle dot, since a row may already hold one under the copy policy; the name keeps its width and the caption truncates first (390 px measured "An…" for Angel before that rule). **Decision:** a grid tile is the printed token with no caption badge, because the badge covered the token's name bar and the printed P/T box and frame already tell the tokens apart. **Decision:** `tokenCaption`, not `idBadge`, because a declared `idBadge` also means "show printed keys" to the Games dialog (`games-dialog.tsx`).
- **"From your cards"** lists the tokens the 100's cards make or use (Scryfall's `all_parts`, ingested as each card's `attrs.tokens`) that the section lacks, each row naming the cards that make it ("Add Soldier (1/1 white), from Elspeth, Sun's Champion"), one click to add, Add all for the rest. The closed heading says how many ("TOKENS 0", then "5 from your cards", no dot between) from the cards the editor already holds, so no request is made until the section opens; opening asks POST `/api/cards/resolve`'s id pass once for the missing keys. **"Add a token"** searches the token catalog alone (GET `/api/cards/search?tokens=1`, the best-match order), each result captioned and previewed on hover. A single add goes through the editor's `handleAdd` (its "Added X" toast and Undo); Add all is one whole-list swap with one Undo, like Import. Tokens never count toward the size, so a full 100 never turns an add into Swap in….
- **Outside the 100 everywhere it matters:** the size, validation, the bracket read, analytics, the export, Suggestions, Autofill (its Apply carries the tokens through), Swap Lab, the Cut Coach, the panels' refetch keys and the combo facts read the playable list (`src/lib/decks/playable.ts`: leader or counted zones, One Piece's leader included); versions, restore, forks, the wire and both views carry the zone. The save route and restore refuse a token in the 100 and a card in Tokens.
- **Measured** (the dev branch after a full Scryfall run with the token catalog: 972 tokens, 3,116 printings, 4,873 linking cards, +2.9 MB; Chrome for Testing through the DevTools MCP, the built-in pane being hidden): a guest deck built with Adeline, Smothering Tithe, Elspeth, Divine Visitation, Sol Ring, Swords and a Plains read Commander, Instant, Artifact, Enchantment, Planeswalker, Land, Tokens; "From your cards" offered Angel, Elspeth's emblem, Human, Soldier and Treasure in one resolve call; Add all saved through one PUT (200) with the deck still 7 / 100, no new combo-facts request and no token in the legality line; after a reload the five tokens, the open section and the Grid view came back; "Add a token" for "soldier" listed twelve Soldiers, each captioned differently; Lighthouse snapshot accessibility 100 with no failed audit on the editor and on the share page with the section open; at 390 with touch no horizontal overflow, the steppers 44 × 44 and the disclosure and field 44 px tall.
- **The token search reads a token's words** (follow-up, 2026-10-24, the owner's report: the Tarkir: Dragonstorm Commander Elemental, 4/4 blue-red, couldn't be found). "elemental" matched 39 tokens, the box showed twelve, and that one came thirtieth. Now the adapter reads the box (`tokenQuery`, Magic's `mtgTokenQuery`): a P/T, color words and keywords become the search's own filters and the rest is the name, so "Elemental 4/4 blue red" finds that one alone, and the placeholder says so ("Name, P/T or colors: Elemental 4/4 blue red", `display.tokenSearch`). A broad search says "Showing 12 of 39." with how to narrow it, under a "Show 12 more" button, and namesakes read by P/T, then colors: tokens are unranked, so the best-match order had left them in oracle-id order. Measured on the dev branch: the narrowed search returned the one token; the broad one paged 39 unique rows in four pages with that Elemental 17th; in the editor it went into Tokens with the deck still 2 / 100, took the TTDC #27 printing and came back after a reload.
- **Left for later:** rows 320 to 330 (One Piece's order, multi-type groups, tokens in export and import, precons' box tokens, the purge's "empty", a tokens-only deck's View control, helper cards on non-token layouts, suggestions that follow Scryfall's printed token, One Piece's 51-card count off the editor, the token search's order by use, a card-search pointer to the Tokens section).

### The Tokens list fills itself from the deck's cards (TOKEN-FILL, 2026-10-24, the owner's ask; `TOKEN-FILL-session-prompt.md`)

- **Derived, never stored.** The Tokens section shows the tokens the deck's cards make (Scryfall's links, `attrs.tokens`) as part of the list, without a click, on the editor and on every share page — precons included, so "Calling All Angels" now lists its Angels, Clue, Spirit and The Monarch. One pure module (`src/lib/decks/token-fill.ts`) derives the set over the playable list; the editor hydrates the token cards through one resolve call when the section opens, the share page loads them in one statement with the hidden ones already out (`token-fill-load.ts`).
- **Two kinds of row, told apart.** The player's own tokens (the search's adds) are rows of the zone with a count, steppers and remove, and read first. Under them an eyebrow, "From your cards", and the derived rows: the name and caption as the text view sets them, no count (the count column is left empty, not zero), the makers right-aligned and muted ("from Dockside Extortionist, Smothering Tithe"), and in the editor a Hide (an eye-off icon in the row's reveal slot, like Remove). In the grid the derived tiles follow the deck's own with no ×N badge. A stored row of the same key supersedes the derived one, so a token never reads twice. The heading counts both.
- **Hidden, not gone.** Hide persists on the deck (`decks.hidden_tokens`, its own PATCH body, never a bump of `updated_at`, never copied on fork) and the hidden tokens the cards still link show as chips under the rows — "Hidden: Treasure", each a Show, plus Show all — so the choice is visible and reversible without a trick. A hidden key whose makers left the deck shows nothing. The share page applies the owner's choices on the server: the pod sees what the owner sees, and a visitor's wire never carries the keys.
- **Copy.** The empty section now reads "No tokens yet. The ones your cards make show up here; they don't count toward the deck's size." The "N from your cards" heading hint is gone: the tokens are in the list, and the count says it.

### What a target pick changed, said where it happens (TARGET-CHANGES, 2026-10-24, the owner's ask; `TARGET-CHANGES-session-prompt.md`)

- **The ask, and what it really asked.** "When you set a target bracket the changes that it makes pops up and tells you what it auto changed." A target never edits the list: it moves which findings the read marks above it, what the tools that follow it leave out, and what the deck says where others see it. Every reader of `targetLevel` was traced (the read's `conflicts`, the bracket line, `applyGoals`, `shellGoals`, the alternatives, `comboBadge`, the share page, the tiles, the link preview, the Share dialog's line) and none writes `deck_cards`, so the callout leads with that: "Your cards didn't change."
- **A callout under the choice, not a toast or a second dialog.** Picking a level pops it in under Your target's `Segmented` (U2's `dw-in`, motion-safe only; it scrolls itself into view in the phone Drawer, focus staying on the choice). It says, in order: the title ("Target set: Bracket 3 (Upgraded)", "Target changed from Bracket 2 to Bracket 4", "Target cleared") with Undo after it; "Your cards didn't change."; the list against the new target in the conflict callout's own words ("1 thing in the list puts the deck above it. See Above your target below.", "Nothing in the list puts the deck above it (before, 1 did)."); what the level allows, as the adapter's guide rows verbatim (all of them for a first target, only the rows that differ on a change: "Game Changers: No limit (was None)", none when cleared); which tools follow it, only those the adapter declares, by the names the editor shows them under ("Suggestions, Autofill and Alternatives leave out cards above it, and the Combos tab marks combos above it."); and where it shows, by `targetShown`'s rule (link previews only when the deck isn't private).
- **Computed, not described.** One pure core module (`src/lib/brackets/target-change.ts`) turns the pick into a change: the count is `read.conflicts.length` of the read the sheet renders after the pick, beside the count captured at the pick; tests pin it against the real Magic `assess` for every pick between Not set and 1 to 5. The callout renders only while the target is the one the pick set, so a pick the goals never took says nothing.
- **Safe to try.** Undo puts the previous target back through `withTarget` on the goals as they are then (answers and exceptions untouched); Dismiss takes the callout away; both hand focus back to the choice. The level already set does nothing. Only a short status (the title and "Your cards didn't change.") is announced; the callout itself is a labelled group. It stays until dismissed, replaced, or the sheet closes.
- **Deviations from the session prompt.** The prompt named the tools "Swap Lab" and "Combo Radar"; no screen shows those names, so the callout says "Alternatives" (the Card tab's section, with its Swap… doors) and "the Combos tab". The prompt's count noun became "things", the word the conflict callout already uses ("These put the deck above it"), since "finding" appears in no rendered copy. No card list can prove Bracket 5 (cEDH is the player's call), so the engine pins run fixture lists that read 1 to 4 against every target.
- **Measured in the pane** (a precon draft, Abzan Armor, at least Bracket 3 with one Game Changer): set 2, change to 4, Undo back to 2 (focus on "2", the row holding `{targetLevel: 2}`), cleared, set 1 at 375 px and 320 px (the callout inside 16 px gutters, its Undo and Dismiss 44 by 44 under touch, no sideways scroll), and change 1 to 3 in the light theme.

### A card search in the header, on every page (HEADER-SEARCH, 2026-10-24, the owner's ask; `HEADER-SEARCH-session-prompt.md`; LATER row 111's first half; the commit after `089f7b0`)

- **What it is.** `HeaderSearch` (`src/components/search/header-search.tsx`) puts X2's `NameSuggest` over every card inside a real GET form to `/cards` (one search landmark, "Cards", around one combobox, "Search cards"): suggestions from two letters, each row opening its card page, and Enter with nothing highlighted, or the "Show every card matching" row, opening the full results. The form works before hydration. Nothing is asked until the reader types, no request data is read (the island reads the path only, like the nav beside it), and the route table is byte-identical.
- **Phones.** Below md a 44 px search button opens a full-width row laid over the header row (absolute, the same height: the editor's toolbar stays at 57 px), its input focused inside the tap (`flushSync` the open, then `.focus()`), because iOS raises the keyboard only for focus inside the gesture and Base UI's popups move focus a frame later. Close, Esc with the popup shut, and a route change close it, and focus returns to the button; the covered controls are hidden while it's open, so Tab never lands under it. The button takes Appearance's slot: below md the Appearance trigger hides and its items (U4a's `AppearanceItems`) end the phone Menu, so the wordmark keeps LATER row 250's 360 px step. Measured at 390 px with touch: the wordmark row 147.8 px and the cluster 159.7, so the row is 355.5 px and fits from 360 with 4.5 px to spare; with Appearance kept inline beside the button it was 403.5 px, and the wordmark would have had to go on every phone up to 416 px.
- **Tablets.** From md the box sits inline before the account slot, up to 24 rem. Below lg the Appearance label and the account name show as their icons (both were `max-sm:sr-only`), so at 768 px the box keeps its placeholder and `/` keycap: 229 px measured signed out, where the first cut left it 152 px and bare (under 10 rem the placeholder and keycap step aside, a floor no measured row reaches now).
- **Both games.** The box searches the open page's game, read off `data-game` on `#main` or an ancestor when the reader focuses or types (never an element inside it: `/account`'s first one is a deck tile), and Magic otherwise. When that game has no match, `NameSuggest`'s opt-in `fallback` asks the other game once and says so: "No Magic cards match. 8 One Piece cards." (short names; GameSwitch's "Magic: The Gathering" reads badly mid-sentence). Enter and the footer follow the game that answered (a hidden `game=optcg`). Every other `NameSuggest` caller is unchanged.
- **The keyboard.** `/` jumps to the box (and selects its text) from any page but the editor routes, where `/` stays the editor's own search; not while typing, with a modifier, or inside a dialog or menu (`isTextTarget`). The keycap shows only where `/` works, on fine pointers.
- **Weight.** Measured on production builds of `6ade698` and this tree: a static page's scripts went from 385,088 to 408,225 bytes gzipped (+22.6 KiB, 6%; `/privacy`, `/legal` and `/brackets` alike), and `/decks/new` +5.8 KiB, since the editor already loaded most of it. Nearly all of it is Base UI's combobox, which only the indexes loaded before; loading it on first use is LATER row 337. A log of what people search is row 336 (a privacy call).
- **Deviations from the session prompt.** Appearance moved into the phone Menu instead of the wordmark hiding up to 416 px (the first cut's answer to the 390 px overflow); the tablet squeeze, which the prompt didn't foresee, is met by icons below lg; `data-game` is read from `#main` and its ancestors only; the fallback line uses short game names.
- **Measured in the pane** (dev, 2026-10-24): at 390 px the tap focused the input in the same task and "sol ri" listed Sol Ring first, Enter opened `/cards?q=sol+ri` and closed the row; at 1280 px `/` focused the box without typing a slash, "luffy" on home made exactly two requests (Magic, then One Piece) and set `game=optcg`; on `/leaders` (768 px) "zoro" asked One Piece once with no fallback line; on the editor `/` focused the editor's "Card search", not the header's; a row click opened Rhystic Study's page; 320 px had no sideways scroll and the Menu listed Dark, Light, System and Background art; both themes.

### Swap to fit, the fewest swaps to a target (BRACKET-FIT, 2026-10-24, the owner's ask; `BRACKET-FIT-session-prompt.md`; the commit after `25b9749`)

- **The ask, built as the fewest swaps.** "Once you set a target bracket after your deck is built can we have a button to swap all the suggested cards all at once to make it that desired bracket." The callout's per-card Swap… buttons name every card a finding holds, but the read's own lines already say the minimum ("Remove 1 of them to fit Bracket 3", "Remove A or B to fit Bracket 2"): swapping every listed card changes the player's deck for nothing. So the one button proposes the fewest swaps, each card out replaced by one that shares its job, and the deck keeps its size.
- **The door.** "Swap to fit Bracket 2…" at the head of Your target's conflict callout, wherever the adapter declares `brackets.fixes` and Swap Lab and the read has a conflict. Until 2026-10-27 it also needed a card that could go out, and the owner, setting a target on a deck whose only Game Changer had no community roles (Seedborn Muse in Abzan Armor), found no button and no word why; now it always shows, and when no card can go its sheet leads with "None of the cards that put the deck above Bracket 2 can be swapped here, but you can still remove or replace them yourself.", lists each card's reason under "What swaps can't fix", asks nothing, and only closes ("Done", no "Swap 0 cards"). The per-card Swap… stay. It closes the Why sheet the way Swap… does; focus comes back to "Why?" (or to the bracket line while the new list's facts are checked). Dialog slot `"target-fit"`, the Fit sheet's shell: the full-height Drawer on phones, the wide Modal from md.
- **What each conflict needs is the adapter's.** `BracketsMeta.fixes(read, input)` returns `{factor, remove, from}` per conflict: at least `remove` copies of `from` must leave. Magic's: the Game Changers past the target's allowance, every clear land-denial copy (Brackets 1 to 3), every extra-turn copy (Bracket 1), one non-commander piece per combo. A commander is never in `from`; its share is named, never swapped. Pinned against the real `assess` at targets 1 to 3: taking `remove` of `from` for every fix leaves no conflict, and one fewer from any fix leaves its factor. Core names no rule.
- **The plan is the exact minimum.** A pure core planner (`src/lib/brackets/target-fit.ts`): forced cards first, then a greedy (the most unmet need, so a Game Changer that is also a combo piece counts twice) whose size bounds an exact branch and bound. The greedy alone missed the minimum on 5 of 4,000 generated instances (by one card each); the planner equals brute force on all of them and on every dev deck at every target. Each card out takes its best-ranked Swap Lab option, judged in its place under the target (none hidden by the goals, none over the per-card budget, no incoming card twice: a matching re-routes before a card goes without). A card with no option is planned around ("Remove A or B": A has none, so B goes).
- **The asks.** Swap Lab's route with its own body (the target rides every ask), plan order, 3 at a time and 9 per open (under a third of the 30-a-minute bucket). A 429 stops the asking and says so; Try again resumes; closing aborts. Opening, asking and reviewing write nothing.
- **The proof inside the product.** The list after the checked swaps goes to `useBracketFacts` (unchanged, one GET for its id set) and the adapter's `assess` with the incoming cards' wires. The footer says "After these swaps, nothing in the list puts the deck above Bracket 2." only when that read finds nothing above the target; otherwise it quotes the read's own sentences. The polite status says the verdict as a whole sentence ("After these swaps, 2 things still put the deck above Bracket 2."). Swap stays the player's call either way.
- **The price of the change.** "The cards coming in cost $8.23.", each card's cheapest printing, the cards the player owns left out (the editor's one owned set: a collection's identities and an owned precon's, PRECON-OWNED).
- **What it can't fix, named.** "What swaps can't fix" lists every card that stays, with its reason: the commander, alternatives not offered (lands), several copies, no community roles yet, no alternative under the goals, kept by the player, or not looked at past the ask limit ("Didn't look at 2 more cards. Swap these first, then open this again for more."). When a fix can't be fully met, the plan still swaps what it can and names the rest, so the player can finish by hand (the owner's call after the ship, 2026-10-24: keep it so, rather than swap nothing when the target is out of reach).
- **Apply.** `fitList` in place (each new card where its old one was, every other row untouched), ONE `applyListSwap`, "Swapped N cards" with one Undo for the batch. A draft mints one row carrying the target.
- **Measured.** The dev corpus (211 Commander decks: 182 precons, 29 user decks), a flagless stand-in for every card that can go: fixes equal `read.conflicts` every time, and `assess` on the after-list leaves exactly the fixes the plan named short; at target 2, 42 decks are above it, 25 fit fully and 17 keep a named fix. Through the real Swap Lab, in process: 19 of the 29 precons past Bracket 2 fit at target 2 (28 asks, at most 3 per precon, 21 distinct cards in); every miss is a card with none of Swap Lab's 17 roles or a land; none of the 21 incoming cards is a Game Changer, by our flags and by Scryfall's live `is:gamechanger` search (53 cards). The editor's client chunks grew by about 11.4 KiB gzipped with PRECON-OWNED (722,563 to 734,214 bytes over every chunk); the route table is byte-identical.
- **In the pane and the simulator** (dev, 2026-10-24): Everyone's Invited! at target 2 (7 conflicts: a Game Changer and six two-card combos) planned 4 swaps for $8.23 and named Arcane Adaptation (no roles) and The World Tree (a land) as what swaps can't fix; Peer Through Time fit with one (Cyclonic Rift for Ugin's Binding, $0.78), and its Undo restored the list and "You own 100/100 · nothing missing"; at 320 px nothing in the Drawer overflowed and the door, every option row and Swap measured 44 px under touch. In the iOS Simulator (iPhone 15, Mobile Safari) Timey-Wimey's sheet swapped Farewell for Austere Command ($0.33) and the line then read "Your target: Bracket 2 · the cards say at least 2".
- **Deviations from the session prompt.** An exact search replaces the greedy alone (the brief's bar was the exact minimum); a card answered with no option is never a candidate rather than last in the tie order; "Coming in for X." sits on its own line (Fit's inline "· picked for X" risked sideways scroll at 320 px); the status sentence and the owned set were wired at integration.

### "Do you own this precon?" (PRECON-OWNED, 2026-10-24, the owner's ask; `PRECON-OWNED-session-prompt.md`; migration `0021`; the commit after `5c3815f`)

- **The ask, and the gap under it.** "If you are starting from a precon it asks Do you own this deck? so that it can calculate the price once you make changes and it would minus the cost since you own the whole precon." The precon draft forgot its precon at its first save (the create carried the name only), so nothing could ask later or change the answer. The deck now remembers it.
- **The deck remembers its precon.** `decks.precon_deck_id` (uuid, a foreign key to decks ON DELETE SET NULL) is set once by the server: from the create's `fromPrecon` slug, which a precon draft's first create always carries whatever edit mints it, or by a fork of a precon. A fork of a user's deck inherits nothing. `decks.owns_precon` (boolean, NULL = not asked) is the answer, saved through its own PATCH body `{ownsPrecon}`, which never moves `updated_at`; on a draft the answer is the edit that mints the row, in its create.
- **One line, under the leader zone.** "Started from Breed Lethality, a precon. Do you own it?" with Yes and No; answered, "Started from Breed Lethality, a precon you own." with Change, which asks again in place. The name is an apposition because some products begin with "The". The line arrives with the list (the owner's GET, or the draft's own seed), so nothing on screen moves; its controls sit after a gap, underlined, 44 px on touch; focus follows the answer.
- **A Yes counts copies, not identities.** The precon's own quantities are owned at $0 in the ownership line (shown with a collection or an owned precon), every goals line's estimate, the Fit sheet and Swap to fit's price line, which all take one owned set. 14 Forests from a 12-Forest precon cost two; a card cut and added back is owned again; the commander counts like any card. The rows' "In your collection" mark stays the collection's.
- **Private.** Only the owner's GET carries `deck.precon` (`{slug, name, owns, cards}`, one indexed read of the precon's counted cards); `deckMetaJson` names neither column, so the share page, link previews and every other wire stay as they were. The precon's row id never leaves the server.
- **The price line wraps on phones** (integration): at 320 px the truncated line hid its end, the price ("nothing missi…"); below md it now wraps, and from md it still truncates where the row must not wrap.
- **Measured.** All 182 dev precons read "You own 100/100 · nothing missing" on Yes. Three staples swapped into Breed Lethality read "missing ≈ $149" ($149.10), the sum of their `cheapest_usd`, equal to the lowest `prices.usd` Scryfall's API gave for each (Cyclonic Rift $29.57, Rhystic Study $66.76, Smothering Tithe $52.77). The No estimate equals `/precons`' figure on 182 of 182 and the share page's "est. price today" on 179 (the share page rounds a float sum down on three half-dollar totals: LATER row 348). In the pane: a precon draft answered Yes minted one row with one POST (`fromPrecon` and `ownsPrecon` in the create) and one PUT; No and Change each sent one PATCH; Rhystic Study in for one card read "You own 99/100 · missing ≈ $67". `pnpm smoke:decks` passed its precon chain (create, owner GET, PATCH, visitor GET, a deck from no precon) on the dev branch.
- **Production migration** (the owner's yes in chat, 2026-10-24): applied through Neon's SQL editor before the push, in one transaction (the two `ADD COLUMN`s, the foreign key, the journal row); read back: both columns nullable, the key `ON DELETE SET NULL`, 0 of 203 decks set, journal id 22 (hash `63ea4e5c…`), the same row `pnpm db:migrate` wrote on the dev branch.

### Upgrade toward a target, Suggestions in (BRACKET-UP, 2026-10-27, the owner's ask; `BRACKET-UP-session-prompt.md`; the commit after `b34957b`)

- **The ask, BRACKET-FIT's upward twin.** "Why cant the button show up if i want to upgrade a precon to bracket 4 and have the whole suggested list apear for the swap button." Swap to fit only fixes cards above a target, so a precon reading Bracket 2 aimed at Bracket 4 had no button. The approved shape: "an Upgrade toward Bracket N button for when the target is above what the cards show. The Suggestions list comes in, each card paired with the weakest card it replaces, in one review sheet with the check of the list after, the cost of the new cards, and one Undo."
- **The door.** "Below your target" in the conflict callout's place, when the target is set, nothing is above it, and the target is above the band the read's floor (`read.suggested ?? read.minimum`) is in, by the adapter's own `bands` (`belowTargetBand`, `src/lib/brackets/target-bands.ts`). A list nothing flags reads "1 to 2", so a target of 2 is met (integration: the subagent's floor rule offered it an upgrade). A pending call is no floor: on "Bracket 3 or 4 · one combo is your call" the door toward 4 shows, and the box says an answer can raise the read (integration, a second pass: a gate on every band the line names hid the door on 17 of the 181 dev precons below 4 with no word why, the way Swap to fit's door once hid itself). A read whose line names no level shows no box: a list under the format's size, or one blocked by a card the format doesn't allow (2 dev precons), whose line already says what it needs first. The box reads "Your target is Bracket 4 (Optimized). The cards show at least Bracket 3 (Upgraded)." (what the cards and the answers say when answers raised the read; "Nothing in this list raises the bracket." with nothing flagged), then "An answer under Your call can raise it." while a call is open ("Nothing in this list raises the bracket unless an answer under Your call does." when nothing else is flagged), then "Upgrade toward Bracket 4…". The box shows only with its door, which the editor passes where the adapter declares Swap in's swap and cuts (One Piece: none). It closes the Why sheet the way Swap to fit does; focus comes back to "Why?" (or to the bracket line while the new list's facts are checked). Dialog slot `"target-up"`, the Fit shell.
- **The asks.** ONE `POST /api/recommendations` with the editor's live list, the deck's goals and its per-card budget goal (the panel's 25 rows), for a saved deck as for a draft, since the GET would read a target the autosave may not have written yet; the route's doc comment says so, its behavior is unchanged. ONE `/api/cards/resolve` for the names, with the panel's id guard: a name that resolves to another card drops its row, and the sheet says so. A 429 says so with Try again. Opening, asking and reviewing write nothing.
- **The pairs.** A pure core planner (`src/lib/brackets/target-up.ts`) over Swap in's machine. Each card, in Suggestions' order, takes the Cut Coach's cheapest ordinary cut at its mana value, the best-ranked card coming in taking the weakest card; once every row has had its own, a card with none left takes the weakest ordinary cut at another cost, and its row says so ("No suggested cut at mana value 2, so the weakest card at another cost goes."): Suggestions fill the curve's gaps, so with its own cost alone two rows in three came back unpaired. And the plan cuts on its own only from the deck's weaker half by the Coach, or its weakest as many as the rows that pair when that is more (integration: on Breed Lethality at target 4, Carrion Feeder came in for Sol Ring, the 1-drop slot holding nothing weaker once Viscera Seer took Hardened Scales; now the 25 cuts are the precon's filler, the read and the price the same). Never the commander, a land (but the player's own pick for a land coming in), a complete-combo piece, a card the Coach can't rank, a card coming in, or one card twice. The chooser offers the card's cost first, then the rest, in the Coach's order; a card another row holds shows as "Mind Stone (out for Arcane Signet)", and taking it re-plans that row, unless that row's own choice holds it. The player's choices win and never move; unchecking a row never moves another row's card.
- **What starts checked.** A paired row, in rank order, unless the adapter's `impact` says its card would put the list above the target once the checked rows above it are in; such a row starts unchecked with the judge's reasons. Suggestions are ranked one card at a time, so with every row checked, precons with no Game Changer would have taken up to 8 of them at a target of 3, and 83 of the 151 would have gone above it. The judgement is live (unchecking a Game Changer above lets the next one in), and the player's own check wins.
- **The proof inside the product.** The list after the checked swaps goes to `useBracketFacts` (one GET for its id set) and the adapter's `assess` with the incoming cards' wires. The footer says "After these swaps, the cards show at least Bracket 4 (Optimized). That's your target." only when that read reaches the target, with the read's own sentences for the floor; short of it, "Cards can only show a floor. If the deck plays at Bracket 4 (Optimized), say so under How it plays." (no list proves cEDH); anything that would put the deck above the target, a combo two cards coming in complete together included, is named in the read's sentences, with a whole-sentence verdict for screen readers.
- **Price and apply.** BRACKET-FIT's price line over the editor's one owned set ("The cards coming in cost $1,853.32.", Goblin Storm's upgrade at 4; `costOf` and the Fit shell are exported from the Swap to fit sheet). Upgrades run past $1,000, so the line groups thousands since integration, for Swap to fit too. `fitList` in place (each new card where its old one was, every other row untouched), ONE `applyListSwap`, "Swapped N cards" with one Undo for the batch. A draft mints one row carrying the target.
- **Measured.** The dev corpus, 182 precons through the real engine in process (Suggestions with the target's goals, the resolve's first pass, the sheet's own plan and judge, `fitList`, then `assess` with the after list's complete combos), at the shipped door: it shows on 151 precons at target 3 and 179 at target 4 (the other two below each are blocked by a card the format doesn't allow; 6 and 17 of those shown have an open call). At 3, 149 reach Bracket 3 after 23.3 swaps on average and none goes above it (the judge leaves 1.4 rows unchecked on average, up to 8; with every row checked, 83 would have gone above), and the two Breed Lethality precons stay at 2 with no Game Changer coming in. At 4, 112 reach Bracket 4 after 24.7 swaps, and the other 67 show at least 3 and say so. Every cut is distinct and never a commander, land, combo piece or one of EDHREC's top 100; no list changed size or land count; only cards outside the curve go unpaired (54 rows at target 3 and 58 at 4: lands, and once a card with no cost). 88% of pairs at target 3 and 83% at 4 cut a card at another cost, and the nonland average mana value fell about 1.1. The cards coming in cost $1,504.71 on average at target 3 and $1,704.13 at 4 (cheapest printings, nothing owned). The production build's route table is byte-identical to `a2224b4`'s (83 lines); client chunks grew about 6.3 KiB gzipped (734,575 to 741,056 bytes over every chunk, at `6fe41a0`).
- **In the pane and the simulator** (dev, 2026-10-27): Breed Lethality at target 2 shows no box (it reads "1 to 2"); at target 4 the door opened a sheet of 25 rows (no cut is Sol Ring), the footer read "After these swaps, the cards show at least Bracket 4 (Optimized). That's your target." with its two fast combos, and the cards coming in cost $153.47; Swap made one edit ("Swapped 25 cards · Undo", still 100 cards) and Undo restored the list. Goblin Storm ("Bracket 3 or 4 · one combo is your call") at target 4 shows the box with "An answer under Your call can raise it.", and its sheet reached 4 in 24 swaps through a Mycosynth Lattice and Vandalblast combo (land denial) for $1,853.32. Party Time ("Bracket 1 to 2 or 4 · one card is your call") shows no box at 2; at 4 it says "Nothing in this list raises the bracket unless an answer under Your call does.", and answering its call No leaves "The cards and your answers say Bracket 1 (Exhibition)." with the door. At 320 px nothing overflowed and the door, the checks, every chooser and Swap measured 44 px under touch. In the iOS Simulator (iPhone 15, Mobile Safari) the box, the sheet and iOS's own grouped picker for the cut chooser.
- **Deviations from the session prompt.** The other-cost fallback, and the weaker-half rule over it (the prompt's partner was the bucket alone); judged defaults rather than every paired row checked; a card the Coach can't rank is never cut on the plan's own; the door asks for Swap in's cuts too; its gate is the band the floor is in (not the floor alone, and not every band the line names), with a sentence for an open call and no box for a read that names no level; the last cost bucket is said as "mana value 7 or more"; the box shows only with its door; the price line groups thousands.
- **Power first** (the owner's call after the ship, 2026-10-27: "Yes, power first"; LATER row 353): at a target above what the cards show, the cards the game counts toward it lead the upgrade. The adapter declares them (`BracketsMeta.toward(read, input, target)`: the factor, how many more, which cards count, and their words, beside BRACKET-FIT's `fixes`). Magic's are Wizards' Game Changers, the one way the cards alone show a higher bracket without mass land denial or a combo whose speed only the player can answer: one for Bracket 3 and four for Bracket 4 (a target of 5 asks for the same four, since no list proves cEDH), less the ones the list holds, its commander's included; Bracket 2 allows none, so nothing leads there. Suggestions take `powerFirst` (the POST body; `RecommendOptions`): the lead comes from every card the goals keep, not the 25 best (at a target of 4 the 25 best held enough Game Changer spells for 90 of the 179 dev precons the door shows on, the 50 best for 171, the whole list for all 179), each one the curve buckets, since a land can't come in for a card at its cost; then the rest in rank order, still 25 rows and one request. The sheet says "Game Changers come first: Bracket 4 (Optimized) needs 3 more." (or how many the Suggestions hold, or none), each lead row says "Counts toward Bracket 4 (Optimized) as a Game Changer.", and the lead pairs first. Measured on the dev corpus at the shipped door (the feeds read as of 14:00 Z that day, before the dev branch's Spellbook feed aged out): at a target of 4 all 179 precons reach Bracket 4 (112 before), the lead averaging 3.9 cards and never short of what the target needs, 4.6 Game Changers coming in on average; at 3 all 151 reach Bracket 3 (149 before: the two Breed Lethality precons take one); none goes above either target, every check holds, no top-100 staple is cut, and the cards coming in average $1,504.67 at 3 and $1,749.26 at 4 ($1,704.13 before). In the pane: Counter Blitz (FINAL FANTASY X), one Game Changer held and none among its 25 best Suggestions (the first at 47th), led with Enlightened Tutor, Mana Vault and Mystical Tutor and reached Bracket 4 ("4 Game Changers…", with a fast combo its Suggestions completed) for $453.11; at 320 px both new lines wrap and nothing overflows.

### Home's opening in the owner's chosen look (FRESH-UI-HOME, 2026-10-30, the owner's ask; `FRESH-UI-HOME-session-prompt.md`; the commit after `3815f98`)

- **Amends `WAVE5.md` D6 section 1 (the split hero and the fan) and U5's "The fan's geometry", and nothing else D6 says.** The owner commissioned a fresh UI from a Codex session on 2026-10-30 (four generated references; an implementation left uncommitted in a worktree, preserved as `caf788f` on `codex/fresh-ui-2026-10-30`), then on 2026-10-30 pointed at the home reference: "this specific look I like". The opening is that look, built at the source: a full-bleed band (`.home-band`, a cast of the primary green over the page's background, deeper at the top and still greener than the page at its lower edge, so the edge reads) with the H1 at the left and the day's three commanders standing upright at the right on a green-lit stage (`.home-fan` keeps its name and slot: a three-column grid whose boxes come from `aspect-ratio`, so nothing shifts when the cards land; the glow is the grid's own `::before`, a radial gradient of the primary at `--home-stage` inside the grid's stacking context, so it never paints over the text; the band clips it, since it overhangs the grid by 12 % and widened the document to 1,320 px at 1280 before the clip); under the band the doors row (`.home-doors`: the `hero` Button size, 56 px with a 16 px label and the arrow as `data-icon="inline-end"`, the width of its column, to `/decks/new`; a ghost "Browse commanders" with an arrow; and `nav[aria-label="Choose your game"]` with a tile per game, each a field of its accent over the card tone, 12 % at rest and 18 % on hover, `data-game` setting `--accent-game`, the serif name on `.type-section`, the format line on `.type-label-sm`, DeckTile's 2 px lift, to that game's draft); then the Magic region in its gallery form (`GameCard headline`: "Find your next commander." on `.type-headline`, the blurb, the six cards, then the region's four doors with their hrefs byte-identical), the One Piece field as U5 left it, and D6's hairline rhythm from the field on. ### The Codex editor half, reconciled at the source (FRESH-UI-EDITOR, 2026-10-31, the owner's ask through LATER row 373's triage; `FRESH-UI-EDITOR-session-prompt.md`; the commit after `e8354cf`)

- **Amends nothing recorded; it adds three things and measures five.** The Codex session's editor half (`caf788f` on `codex/fresh-ui-2026-10-30`) was a 453-line `@layer utilities` skin keyed on `aria-label`s and element structure. Its three ideas worth keeping are rebuilt on the elements they style, with tests; its five contested changes (each a reversal of a recorded decision: U3a's rows and placeholders, D0 / F4's eyebrow, U1 / U10a's control heights, U3b's clear panes over R2's art, the Geist name) are measured below through an injected stylesheet on the Giada draft and put to the owner in the prompt's ship note, none shipped; the rest (the opaque panes, the header and the search block without `--chrome`, the skin itself, the `hero` and `comfortable` Button sizes on the editor, the capability-sentence rewrite, the `data-slot` hooks for CSS, the left-aligned empty state, the phone rows' wrapping names) is dropped. No migration, no route, no dependency; every pinned string, `aria-label` and the doors' order and hrefs unchanged; new copy is "Find a card" alone.
- **The search pane has a title.** `<h2>Find a card</h2>` heads the sticky block, in the deck pane's heading class (`text-sm font-semibold`, Geist 14 / 600) inside a 32 px row (`flex h-8 items-center`, `mb-1`), so its text sits on the "Deck" heading's line: both measured at 132 to 150 px from the top at 1440 and 159 to 177 at 768 (the deck pane's summary row is the ring's 32 px; a bare 20 px line sat 6 px high). Below `md` it is `sr-only`, U4a's rule for "Deck": the tab bar's "Search" is the title and the phone's sticky block keeps its 84 px. The input moves from y 125 to 161 at 1440 (152 to 188 at 768), the sticky block from 142.61 to 178.61 px, and the results in view at the first screen from 15 to 14 (1440) and 17 to 16 (768). Lighthouse's heading-order audit passes before and after (desktop and mobile; accessibility 100, best practices 96 with the known console-CSP failure, SEO 63 for the editor's own `noindex`); the editor has no `h1` (the name is an input), so the new `h2`, first in DOM order, skips nothing. One Piece's single-panel "Card" heading keeps R3's 40 px strip row (its text at 123.5 to 141.5 px): the deck and search titles share a line, the tools strip sits 9 px higher, as it did against "Deck" before.
- **A result row is a grid, and the selected row's actions take a second line.** `grid-cols-[1.625rem_minmax(0,1fr)_auto]`: the thumb, the name (`truncate`), and one meta cell holding `×N` and the pips (`data-slot="search-meta"`); the actions (`data-slot="search-actions"`) stay `hidden` until a state shows them and, when hover shows them inline, take an implicit fourth column, so an unselected row on a fine pointer is byte-identical to before (44 px; at 1440 the name 224 × 20 at x 42, the pips at x 274; 256 px wide at 768). On the selected row (`group-aria-selected/row:`) and on every row on a coarse pointer (where R4 shows the actions always, because a tap is an inspection), the actions move to a second row under the name, spanning the name and meta columns and right-aligned (`col-start-2 col-end-4 row-start-2 justify-self-end`), the thumb spans both rows (`row-span-2`) and the row gains `gap-y-1`; the name keeps its line with its meta, so the pip column is where every other row has it. Measured at 1440 fine: the selected row 56 px (44 before), its name 245 px wide and whole where it was 35 px and truncated ("Sol Ring" cut beside 161 px of actions); at 768, 235 px (67 before). At 390 with touch every row is 76 px (52 before), the names 274 to 295 px wide where they had 90 to 111, 0 of 20 truncate, and 7 rows fit the first screen where 11 did (the owner kept the two-line rows the same day: "Keep 76 px rows"; LATER row 377 closed). Hover never changes a row's layout: a layout that moved under the pointer would change the row under it. Selection moving by arrow re-lays only the two rows that change state; `scrollIntoView` keeps the row in view as before.
- **One primary door in the empty state.** With no leader, "Add cards" is the default variant (the only `bg-primary` in the row) and the Y2b doors, "Paste a list", "Start from a precon" and "Surprise me", are `ghost` (muted ink, 8.57:1 on charcoal); once a leader is set, "Autofill a starter shell" is the primary (W9b: the shell is built around a leader), "Add cards" is the outline beside it as it was, and "Paste a list" is a ghost; One Piece shows Add cards primary and Paste a list ghost. Measured on the fresh draft at 1440 and at 390 with touch (every door 44 px, the row two lines at 96 px) and after "Surprise me" (one GET, nothing written) in both trees; `deck-editor.test.tsx`'s `emptyActions()` order and text pins hold unedited.
- **The owner's call, measured here and decided by the owner ("Keep as recorded", 2026-10-31)** (Chrome for Testing through the DevTools MCP, dev, the Giada draft `/decks/new?game=mtg&from=calling_all_angels_fdc`, an injected `<style>` per candidate that is never committed; `.claude/fresh-ui-editor-evidence/oc-*.js.txt`): (1) **16 px names in 40 px rows** (U3a: 14 in 28): rows in the deck pane's first screen 10 to 7 at 1440 and 12 to 9 at 768; the pane's scroll height 2,892 to 3,708 px (1440) and 2,926 to 3,742 (768); the name line 20 to 22.86 px; the group placeholder's `1.75rem` constant would move to `2.5rem` (touch rows are 48 px already). (2) **Sentence-case 15 px 600 labels** (D0 / F4: the uppercase 12 px eyebrow names list groups, the Wave-5 addendum's "Share and editor groups keep the 12 px label"): label widths 87 / 63 / 56 / 51 / 55 / 96 / 36 px against 82 / 64 / 58 / 50 / 57 / 92 / 33, the 46 px heading unchanged; a policy reversal, not a layout one. (4) **A card-toned center pane** (R2's art shows through the clear panes at 8 % dark / 4 % light): opaque `--card` hides the art behind the deck entirely and lifts the pane's lowest text contrast from 8.57 to 7.78:1 on charcoal (muted ink on `#171c1e`, still a pass) and from 4.89 to 5.28:1 on ivory (the Warden line on `#fffdf8`); a 60 % oklab mix leaves 40 % of the art's presence (3.2 % dark, 1.6 % light), 8.06:1 and 5.12:1; screenshots in both themes beside the baseline. ### The stage and the painted doors (HOME-ATMOSPHERE, 2026-10-31, the owner's ask on the Codex home reference; the commit after `1d6583b`)

- **Amends FRESH-UI-HOME's "what the reference invents" (the tiles' landscape art and the green lighting) and nothing else it decided.** The owner's prompt of 2026-10-31 named three visual elements from `~/Documents/codex/Deckwarden/fresh-ui/references/home.png` and made the reference the visual authority: the dark green, lit, textured setting behind the three hero cards; the fantasy landscape behind the Magic door; the ocean and sailing ship behind the One Piece door. The reference was separated into what it is made of: the stage is CSS (light follows the cards at every width), the texture is one small SVG tile, the three cards stay the day's real Scryfall `normal` cards through CardImage (the pictured identities were never required), and the two paintings are the reference's own tile artwork with its labels and marks painted out, with the words as HTML over them. Everything else on the page is untouched: the H1 and sentence, the 56 px Build bar, the gallery, the routes, the card sourcing and attribution.
- **The stage** (`.home-band`, `.home-fan` and their pseudo-elements in globals.css; the tokens `--home-cast`, `--home-stage`, `--home-floor`, `--home-grain`, `--home-shade`, `--home-halo`, `--home-contact`, `--home-grain-url`, each set once per theme on the band): the band's cast falls to a hair of green at its edges (dark: the primary at 10 % → 6 % over the page, a 1 px dark line at its foot, the table's edge); the lit wall is the fan's `::before`, a `closest-side` radial from `--home-stage` (dark `oklch(0.365 0.034 166)`, the reference's desaturated forest green, not the primary) centered a little above the cards' middle, with the grain blended in (`background-blend-mode: soft-light`) and the whole masked to the same falloff so no edge shows; the lit floor is the fan's `::after`, a `closest-side` pool of `--home-floor` (`oklch(0.46 0.046 172)`) in a 10rem box starting 5rem above the cards' bases and running under the band's clip, masked the same way; the grain (`public/home/stage-grain.svg`, 865 B, a 240 px `feTurbulence` tile) also lies faintly over the whole band on its own `::after`. Each card is grounded by box-shadows on `.home-fan > *` (0 20px 16px −4px of `--home-shade`, a 8 px halo, a 2 px contact line; U2's `tokens.test` forbids `drop-shadow` anywhere in globals.css, which the first cut tripped, so these replace U8's `HERO_CARD_SHADOW` utility on the trio, which the stage now lights). The two side cards turn 9° about the vertical axis toward the viewer (`transform: rotateY`, pivoting on the edge nearest the center, `perspective: 1400px` on the grid): the reference's shallow arc. Not an in-plane rotation: a card's footprint only narrows inside its own grid cell, so D0's artist-and-© rule holds by construction as it did upright (`hero-fan.test` still pins no `rotate:`, no drop, no fan step). The band's padding tightens to 3rem / 2.5rem from `lg` and the fan's to 1rem / 1.5rem, so the band is 427 px tall at 1536 against the reference's 432, and the gap grows to `clamp(0.75rem, 1.8vw, 1.75rem)`.
- **The painted doors** (`.home-tile`, `.home-tile-art`, `.home-tile-arrow`, the `::before` scrim; `TILE_ART` in `page.tsx` holds each file, its pixel size and its `object-position` focus, so a path or a crop changes in one place): the two landscapes are `public/home/mtg-tile.webp` (1,088 × 405, 17,946 B; the castle on its floating island, focus 62 % 50 %) and `public/home/optcg-tile.webp` (1,158 × 405, 26,660 B; the ship between the islands, focus 56 % 45 %), cut from the owner's reference at 435 × 162 and 463 × 162, the planeswalker symbol, the compass, both label lines and the sail's emblem painted out (OpenCV Telea inpainting over glyph and box masks, the script and the before/after crops in `.claude/home-atmosphere-evidence/`), then upscaled 2.5× with a light unsharp mask for 2x screens. A plain decorative `<img alt="">` of the static file (no optimizer; eager, `fetchpriority="low"` so it never competes with the trio for the LCP) fills the tile under a scrim darkest at the left and the foot; the words sit at the foot as the reference has them (the name on `.type-section`, the format line under it, both in `--primary-foreground`'s ivory with a 1 px text shadow, the same in both themes since the painting is), the arrow top-right; the accent field remains the paint behind the art while it loads. The tiles are 10rem tall (the reference's 161 px) and 6.5rem on one column below 40rem; hover scales the art 1.03 over 400 ms under the link's own lift, `prefers-reduced-motion` keeps it still.
- **Measured** (dev `:3000`, dark unless said; exact-pixel captures from Chrome for Testing 155 headless at the reference's 1536 × 1024 and at 1280 × 800, 1024 × 768 and 768 × 1024, the phone in the pane at 375 × 812 with touch; the images, the reference overlay, wipe and stacked comparisons, the color samples and the scripts in `.claude/home-atmosphere-evidence/`). Stage colors, ours against the reference at the same spots: the band's left margin (17, 23, 23) against (16, 21, 22); the wall above the cards (55, 76, 67) against (48, 64, 56); the lit floor beside the cards (55, 78, 71) against (53, 75, 69); the wall right of the trio (26, 32, 32) against (20, 32, 27); the floor's far left (16, 22, 22) against (18, 22, 23); the gap between two cards (23, 35, 29) against (47, 63, 53), the one spot that stays darker (our shadows' halo where the reference's wall is lit through). Geometry: the band 427 px tall at 1536 (432), the cards 204 / 215 / 204 px wide as projected in a 700 px column (the reference's three are about 235 px in a 766 px trio, see below), the tiles 342 × 160 at 1536 (the reference's 434 × 161 in a wider doors row) and 343 × 104 on the phone. Label contrast over the scrimmed painting, computed from the asset and the CSS at every pixel under the words (`tile-contrast.py.txt`): the names at 1536 never under 5.46:1 (One Piece) and 7.37:1 (Magic), the format lines never under 13.1:1; on the 358 px phone tile the names never under 4.97:1, the 5th percentile 6.05:1 and up. CLS 0.0001 at 1536 (one entry) and 0 at 375; no horizontal overflow at either (`scrollWidth` = `innerWidth`); the two WebP files and the SVG each a 200 from the dev server and `complete` with real pixels before the first paint settles; Tab reaches each tile with `:focus-visible` true and the game accent's 2 px ring on a 2 px offset; 0 new targets under 44 px at 375 with touch (the tiles 343 × 104). The light theme carries the same structure in the primary's tints over the paper (the lit wall at 30 %, the floor at 24 %, the grain at 0.12). `pnpm check`: 3,336 tests in 296 files, 0 lint errors, the 6 warnings as before.
- **Pins moved, said here:** `hero-fan.test.tsx` (the stage's two pseudo-elements with their grain and masks, the `box-shadow` on the cards, the side cards' `rotateY` and pivots, and no `shadow-` utility on the trio's images), `page.test.tsx` (each tile's `<img>` of a `/home/…webp` file, decorative, eager and low-priority, and the `--tile-focus` style). Held: every other pin in both files, `tokens.test` (U2's no-filter rule, met with box-shadows), `game-card.test`, smoke:seo's strings.

### HOME-ATMOSPHERE refinement — crisp sails and a continuous stage (2026-10-31)

The owner rejected the blurred ship sails and the shadow treatment behind the three cards, asking to finish the unavailable artwork from the previous session and follow `fresh-ui/references/home.png` more closely.

- Replaced both enlarged screenshot crops with fresh 1600 × 533 paintings generated using the reference. The Going Merry's sail, straw-hat emblem and rigging remain intact and sharp. Labels remain HTML, decorative images stay eager with low fetch priority, and links are unchanged.
- Replaced the radial wall/floor lights and repeated SVG grain with one 2160 × 726 textured wall-and-tabletop asset. The background follows the card grid, fades at its top and sides, and is subdued to 16% over paper in light mode and 78% in dark mode. Headline content stays above the backdrop. Short downward shadows replace the broad halo around each card.
- Allowed the trio to extend up to 80 px into the right desktop margin from 1280 px. At 1536, projected card widths are 228 / 242 / 228 px and the band is 433 px tall. No card overlaps another or crops its artist/copyright line.
- Browser QA: 1536 desktop, 1280 laptop (including light mode), 768 tablet and 390 phone; no horizontal overflow in measured desktop/tablet/phone views, sharp tile art, visible keyboard focus, and no browser console warnings/errors. The two-line hero heading remains. The 390 px tile crop preserves the sail emblem and the ship.
- `pnpm check`: 3,336 tests in 296 files pass, typecheck/format pass, zero lint errors with six existing warnings. The old CSS-string assertions for the discarded lighting implementation were removed; full-card geometry, eager loading, side turns and route tests remain.
- The brighter replacement sail required a stronger left-side scrim. Pixel-composited estimates across the desktop and phone label boxes put the minimum name contrast at 4.82:1 and format-line contrast at 8.58:1. This preserves detail on the ship while supporting the live labels.
- `pnpm build` passes. Asset provenance and generation prompts: `assets/home/README.md`. LATER rows 378 and 379 closed. The prior session summary is retained unchanged as historical context.


### The daylight table (LIGHT-MODE-DAYLIGHT, 2026-10-31, the owner's brief; `~/Documents/codex/Deckwarden/session-handoffs/LIGHT-MODE-DAYLIGHT/START-HERE.md`; the commit after `5d27146`)

The owner likes the dark home's atmosphere and wanted light mode to feel as intentional, and reported that the commander art behind the builder could hardly be seen on light. The brief's direction: a daylight collector's table, with a generated full-page concept as the visual target and a separate empty backdrop as the asset. Everything below is light only. Dark's home and builder, and the share page but for its header search field's fill on light, are pixel-identical to `5d27146` (see Measured).

- **Amends two recorded decisions, on the owner's word, for light only.** R2 §3's "about 4 % in light" for the builder's crop (the share page keeps it), and the "clear panes over R2's art" FRESH-UI-EDITOR kept that same morning: search and tools now carry a translucent tint on light, never an opaque panel; dark's panes stay clear. U3b's chrome, its fallbacks, the 28 px rows, the 36 px input, the row density, the shortcuts, the sheet and the deck's persistence are untouched.
- **The stage** (`.home-band`, `.home-fan::before`): one token set per theme (`--home-stage-image`, `-opacity`, `-inset`, `-position`, `-size`, `-mask`, `-composite`, and `--home-card-shadow`), so `.dark .home-band` is a plain statement of what dark always was. Light is `public/home/hero-stage-light.webp` at full strength, the owner's asset as delivered (2172 × 724, 98,168 B), no card pixels in it. The concept turned out to be our 1440 × 960 render scaled by 1.0667 (its 462 px band is our 433), so 1440 is the frame it was compared at. From `lg` the stage reaches both band edges: its left edge sits a fixed 40rem before the trio (so the ramp, measured in rem from that edge, always lands in the same place against the headline's column), its right inset is `min(-1rem, calc(44rem - 50vw))`, `cover` from the left. The mask is two ideas added together: a slanted ramp for the wall (22rem to 45rem, eased, so the headline stays on ivory) and a band for the table that fades in from the stage's left edge (the table runs further left than the wall, as in the concept, and no box edge shows on a very wide screen). Stacked, below `lg`, the stage starts at the sentence's foot, `auto 116%`, 78 % across, and the wall fades up from the ivory over 44 % of its height with a five-stop ease. The band's cast on light is the page itself, since the asset's left end already is (249,245,238 against 247,244,238). The band's foot on light is its `::after`, above the stage: an inset shadow sits under it.
- **The cards** stay the day's real commanders. On light each casts a short warm shadow toward the lower left (`-9px 9px 13px -8px` and a contact line, browns, not the green-black): the light in the asset comes from the upper right. Geometry, turns, priority and the D0 rule are untouched.
- **The headings** read `--home-heading`: `#123429` on light, 12.4:1 on the page, a touch deeper than the brief's starting `#173C31` because that read lighter than the concept (its glyph fill samples 5,33,29); the foreground on dark. A `text-(--home-heading)` on the h1 and the gallery's h2, not a color on the ladder's classes.
- **The doors**: the paintings are unchanged files. Light's scrim is measured from the words in rem, so it fits the 160 px and 104 px tiles alike: 0.70 at the foot, 0.65 at the top of the name, gone by 6.5rem, and its mask eases from 13.5rem to 16 % at 20rem, so the sky and the sail stay bright to the right of the label. `brightness(1.1) saturate(1.03)` on the art, light only, which is what the concept's brighter sky asked for. The arrow on light is full ivory on a 28 px disc (`rgb(8 12 14 / 0.48)`, a faint ivory ring): it can land on a cloud or the sail, where the bare arrow on dark measures about 1.2:1. The tile's hover now colors the arrow from CSS (`.home-tile:hover`, under `(hover: hover)`, as the utilities did), so `group/tile` is gone. A 228 px tile (1024 to 1035 wide) wrapped "Magic: The Gathering" (191 px) over the scrim's top at 2.1:1; on light the right padding gives way there, since nothing at the foot needs it.
- **The builder's art**: the crop and the fallback gradient are now two tokens (`--ambient-crop-opacity`, `--ambient-gradient-opacity`), and the veil is three (`--ambient-veil-top`, `-middle`, `-foot`), R2's numbers at `:root` and `.dark`. `data-surface="editor"` on EditorLayout's root scopes the builder's light values: crop 14 %, veil 50 / 20 / 65, so 7 %, 11.2 % and 4.9 % of the image down the page where it was 1.2 %, 2.8 % and 0.4 %. The brief's starting 15 % under 50 / 15 / 65 put "Started from…" under the leader card's shadow at 4.33:1 over Sauron; 14 % and a middle of 20 % clear it with the commander still plainly there. The gradient stays 4 %: a deck with no art (One Piece, Off, a failed image) looks as it did. `ambient-strength.test.ts` computes the bound from the tokens (muted text over a black crop holds 4.5:1 up to a 13.2 % contribution) and the floor the other way (over 4× the old middle), so a later tune can't quietly break either.
- **The panes on light**: `--editor-pane-search` (the card at 58 %) and `--editor-pane-tools` (38 %), transparent on `:root` so dark reads nothing. The deck pane stays clear, so the art shows most where the eye rests. Without the tint the search block's 88 % chrome ended in a hard edge across the pane at 15 %; with it the edge is gone at any art. No new active-row token was needed: the search row's `bg-accent` is opaque and clear over every crop checked. `--ambient-credit` is 90 % in the builder on light (80 % everywhere else), so a dark thumbnail scrolling under the attribution leaves its words at 4.5:1. The two primary search fields (the site header's, the builder's) fill with `--card` on light, the brief's "clearer white controls"; every other control keeps its transparent fill, and no border, muted text or focus ring moved.
- **Fallbacks**: `prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce` and `prefers-contrast: more` (one media list, which a browser without the first query still honors for the second) return the crop to 4 %, the veil to 70 / 30 / 90 and the panes to clear, beside the chrome's own solid strip.
- **Measured** (dev and a local production build, `:3000`; exact viewports from a scratch headless Chrome for Testing driven over CDP, its own profile, since the DevTools MCP's was locked by other sessions; zero-write precon and `?leader=` drafts; evidence and scripts in `.claude/light-mode-daylight-evidence/`). Against the concept at 1440, sampled spots, ours then its: behind the h1 247,244,238 / 246,243,235; the wall in the first gap 178,180,155 / 179,180,155; the wall at the top 182,184,158 / 190,190,167; the table right 205,190,171 / 208,195,178. The stage alone at 20 widths from 320 to 2560: behind the h1 within 2.7 levels of the page with a texture deviation under 0.6 (0 and 0 when stacked), the largest column-to-column step 2.6 levels, so no mask edge; the horizon at 58 to 82 % of the card's height. Tile labels at every device pixel under the words, 17 widths from 320 to 1536, hover's 1.03 scale included: names at least 5.18:1, format lines at least 4.98:1, the arrow's strokes at least 3.12:1. Text census of every visible run on the composited pixels (wrong for the palette's `lab()` colors, see "Desktop Safari, read"): home at 1536, 1280, 1024, 768 and 390 no run under 4.5:1 (lowest 5.18, "Free. No account needed." at 1024); the builder over Sauron, Giada, Jared Carthalion, the Sliver Gravemother and Miku, with search results, at 1536, 1440 and 768, and the three tool tabs at 1536: no run under 4.5:1, the lowest 4.56 ("Started from…" over Jared at 768). The phone's deck, search and tools panes read the same (the only flags there were rows under the tab bar and an sr-only heading). CLS 0 at 1440 in both themes and at 390 touch. No horizontal overflow at 24 widths from 320 to 2560; two-line h1, cards inside the band, none overlapping, ratio 0.718 everywhere. Each theme fetches only its own stage, at Low priority, with the three hero cards High. Background art Off, no leader, a blocked crop and a One Piece leader all paint what they did (gradient 4 %, no chip, or nothing). The real Appearance menu flips Dark and Light with no reload, 8 % and 14 % following at once. Reduced motion: no entry animation, the art at 14 %. The Off to On path crossfades (`dw-out` leaving, chip gone at once, `dw-in` back). `/` focuses search, arrows move `aria-selected`, Tab reaches each tile with its ring. No console or CSP message in the production build. **Baseline proof:** a detached worktree of `5d27146` on `:3001` against the final production build, each pair at the same viewport with the Scryfall images hidden (image resampling differs between two runs of the same code: baseline against itself reads up to 3.7 % at the cards' edges): dark home at 1536, 1280, 1024, 768, 390, dark builder at 1536, 1024, 768, 390 and the share page on dark at 1536 and 390: 0.000 % changed beyond 2 levels, but for one 0.005 % patch in the phone header's icons on one run, which appears on other pairs on other runs. On light, the share page at 1536, 768 and 390, `/commanders`, `/c/`, `/tournaments` and `/brackets`: the only box that differs on any of them is the header search field (at most 21 levels, its fill).
- **Pins moved, said here:** `ambient-art.test.tsx` (the two tokens), `hero-fan.test.tsx` (each theme's stage names a file that exists), `editor-layout.test.tsx` (the root's `data-surface`), `page.test.tsx` and `game-card.test.tsx` (the heading's color utility), and the new `ambient-strength.test.ts`. `tokens.test.ts` is untouched: no palette token moved, and the ambient block sits after the chrome's fallbacks so its query still finds theirs first.
- **What stays different from the concept, and why:** the Build bar keeps `#23483C` and its gold hairline where the concept draws a darker fill with none, and it and Browse keep Geist where the concept drew a serif, as the brief asks (the fill near `#23483C`, the interface sans); the concept's page ivory samples 250,247,241, ours stays `#F7F4EE`; the table beside the first card is lighter (212,201,182 against 183,171,147), since the concept's long shadow there is its own and ours are the asset's plus the card's; the Magic painting is a different picture from the concept's, warmer and a little darker at its left (the tile's top half 141,143,136 against 149,157,157), as the brief keeps the files.
- **Real WebKit:** Mobile Safari in the iPhone 15 Simulator against the local production build: the stacked stage, the live theme switch through the phone's menu and the builder's art all render as in Chromium. The iPad, which would reach the `lg` mask, needs the owner's grant to the simulator tool, so a small probe page stood in on the iPhone: the same token-driven three-layer `mask-composite: add, intersect` empties the corner it should. Desktop Safari itself has not been looked at.
- **Production** (`a555343`, pushed 2026-10-31; Vercel success 13:06:31 Z, CI run 41204011839 green 13:11:01 Z; read from outside, page views only): `/home/hero-stage-light.webp` answers 200, `image/webp`, 98,168 B; the live HTML carries `text-(--home-heading)` on both headings; captures at 1536, 1440, 1280, 1024, 768, 390 and 360 on light, 1536, 768 and 390 on dark: the same band, trio and card boxes as the local builds, no horizontal overflow, each theme's own stage (1 and 0.78), the h1 18,52,41 and 243,241,234; the builder's crop 0.14 on light at 1536, 768 and 390 and 0.08 on dark, its credit chip up; no console or CSP message on any of them. The captures and sheets are in `.claude/light-mode-daylight-evidence/after/`.
- **Left for later:** LATER rows 380 (the share page's art on light stays at R2's 4 %) and 381 (dark's tiles, unchanged here, measure low at some crops).

### The limits of the daylight table (DAYLIGHT-LIMITS, 2026-10-31, the owner's word on LATER rows 380 and 381; `DAYLIGHT-LIMITS-session-prompt.md`; the commit after `d3d3645`)

What LIGHT-MODE-DAYLIGHT left open: dark's game tiles read poorly at some widths, the commander behind a public deck page on light couldn't be seen, and no record showed WebKit rendering the `lg` layouts.

- **Amends, on the owner's word.** LIGHT-MODE-DAYLIGHT's "dark is unchanged", for `.home-tile` only: dark's stage, its builder and share art (8 %, the veil at 70 / 30 / 90) and every other dark pixel stay. And R2 §3 a second time, for the share page on light only: "about 4 % in light" becomes 14 % under 50 / 20 / 65, as in the builder, and "behind the share page's main column" becomes behind the page, the layer standing in the viewport while a crop is up. Dark keeps R2's numbers and its deck-tall column layer (LATER row 382).
- **Dark's tiles, the scrim.** The prompt's starting shape, `max(48%, 13.5rem)`, didn't hold: 48 % of a 342 px tile is 164 px, so it moves the wide tile that already passes, and on a 228 px tile the percent stops pushed past the edge lay about 0.4 over the sail from top to bottom. So the two percent layers stay as they were and a third joins them only where the tile is narrow: `.dark .home-tile::after`, a band measured from the words as light's is, 0.44 up to 4.25rem and gone by 6.5rem, its mask easing from 13.5rem to nothing at 20rem. The sail and the sky above the name keep their pixels. Three width ranges, because a tile's width jumps with the layout: a phone under 380 (the tile under 348 px, 104 tall), the stacked pair under 688 (under 320 px, 160 tall), the even split under 1280 (228 to 292 px). A sweep at every pixel set those edges: at 686 the name already reads 5.94, at 684 4.35; on phones 380 reads 4.61 and 379 reads 4.48.
- **Dark's tiles, the arrow and the wrap.** Dark now reads `.home-tile`'s own three arrow tokens, light's: full ivory on the 28 px disc at 0.48 with the faint ring. Judged by eye against 0.38 over the cloud and the sail, 0.48 reads as a glass button, not a sticker, and it's the simpler file. Hover no longer brightens the arrow, since it's already full on both themes, so that rule is gone; the painting's 1.03 scale and the tile's 2 px lift run as they did. The padding rule for 1024 to 1039 loses its `:root:not(.dark)`, so "Magic: The Gathering" sits on one line in both themes.
- **The share page's art.** `data-surface="share"` on the deck's `<main>` (not the unknown-game fallback's), its own block after the builder's and its own fallback entry, as `rule()` in `ambient-strength.test.ts` needs: crop 14 %, veil 50 / 20 / 65, credit 90 %, gradient still 4 %, no pane tokens, since the page has no panes and no chrome strip (it passes no `stickyHeaders`). **The tokens alone didn't show the commander.** The layer is as tall as the deck (3,917 px for The Hosts of Mordor at 1536), so `object-cover` magnifies the 626 × 457 crop 8.6 times to a sliver of the picture; Giada's face was a blur halfway down the page. And it stops at the column's 64rem, a visible rectangle at 14 %. So on light, while a crop is up, the whole layer stands in the viewport: `[data-slot="ambient-art"]:has([data-slot="ambient-crop"])`, `position: fixed`, its height `calc(100lvh + 10rem)` from 5rem above, and `<main>` gives up `isolate` so the art sits behind the site header and footer rather than washing their words out (body's background is the canvas, so a negative z-index stays above it). With no crop the layer is what it was, so a deck with no art paints as it did. By eye the band and the layer don't read as one image doubled: beside the band the layer is about 8 to 10 % of the picture, at another scale.
- **What real WebKit changed in that.** The first shape fixed the crop and the veil separately at plain `100lvh`. On the iPhone, with the address bar collapsed, Safari shows the page through the strips under its status bar and floating toolbar (59 and 58 pt on an iPhone 15), and the art ended there in a faint hard line (238,235,231 against the page's 247,244,238). Running them 5rem past each edge reached the strips, but there WebKit painted the crop without its veil, a step of about 11 levels (223 against 234). One fixed box, the layer, composites them together: the largest row-to-row step down the margin is then 1.27 levels, top to bottom. The document's height doesn't change (4,063 at 1440, 4,526 at 390, and in WebKit the document ends at the footer).
- **Measured, tiles** (local production builds, baseline a detached worktree of `d3d3645` on `:3001`, harness and numbers in `.claude/daylight-limits-evidence/`). Worst device pixel under the words, hover scale on and off. Before: names under 4.5:1 at 1024 to 1279 (2.17 at 1036, 4.23 at 1279), 640 to 684 (3.75 at 640), and under 380 (2.75 at 320, 4.48 at 372 and 379), format lines at least 5.46, the arrow about 1.1 to 1.8. After, 118 widths from 320 to 1920, both tiles: names at least 5.37 on a narrow tile and 4.55 elsewhere (381, whose pixels didn't move), format lines at least 8.40, the arrow at least 3.72, every label on one line. Light unchanged at 5.18, 4.98 and 3.12. Outside the discs, 0.000 % changed beyond 2 levels at 1536, 1440, 1280, 900, 768, 700, 639, 500, 430 and 390. **One passing width did change:** 375 read 4.55 between 372 and 379 at 4.48, so the band covers it, names 5.64 and 4.55 to 10.20 and 8.94. Census of dark's home at 1536, 1280, 1024, 768 and 390, walked from the top through the tiles and the gallery to the footer: no run under 4.5:1, lowest 7.53. Tab reaches both tiles with `:focus-visible` and the ring, in both themes; reduced motion leaves the painting and the tile still.
- **Measured, share page.** Census of every text run over Sauron, Giada, Jared Carthalion, the Sliver Gravemother, Miku and one user's public deck (`xg2_k9mejm4y`, with an author, a description and notes; the dev branch has no public fork, so the fork credit line wasn't on screen) at 1536, 1440, 768 and 390, at the top, into the list and at the analytics: 7,918 runs, lowest 4.70 ("Dominaria United Commander" on Painbow; wrong for the palette's `lab()` colors, see "Desktop Safari, read"). The one flag is the credit chip lying over "Start from this precon" at 390, the same overlap the baseline reads at 1.41 (LATER row 384); the chip's own words there went from 4.31 to clearing 4.5. The strengths down the viewport are 7 %, 11.2 % and 4.9 % by the tokens; with the layer's overshoot the visible top and foot at 1536 × 1024 are about 7.6 % and 5.8 %, under the 13.2 % bound. Forty baseline pairs at 0.000 % beyond 2 levels: dark's share page (top and list at 1536, 768, 390, and the user's deck), dark and light builders, light's home, `/c/` and `/commanders`, and on light a One Piece deck, Background art Off, no leader, a blocked crop and the private gate. One crop request at Low priority and the same `cache-control` in both builds; CLS 0 at 1440 and 390 in both; no horizontal overflow; both credits visible and uncropped, at the top and scrolled. The fallback media give 4 % and 70 / 30 / 90 with the layer still standing in the viewport (R2's numbers, not its sliver). Reduced motion: no entry animation. The Off to On path: `dw-out` with the layer still fixed, chip gone at once, the layer back to its tall box after the fade, `dw-in` on the way back. The Appearance menu moves 0.08 and 0.14 with no reload. Printing is unchanged: five pages, no art in either build.
