### Suggestions and the Cut Coach read what the list is built around (COMMANDER-FIT, 2026-11-16, the owner's ask on a friend's feedback; `a26827e`)

- **The feedback, and what was true.** A friend of the owner's, relayed by the owner: "Under the 'suggestions' and 'cut' categories under tools, Warden's ai seems to prioritize popular/mana-efficient cards as opposed to cards that interact well with your commander. … Sometimes super unpopular cards work really well in your specific deck." There is no model anywhere in the site: both lists are a fixed formula over four signals (EDHREC's rank over all decklists, Topdeck's top-16 lists with the exact commander, Commander Spellbook's combos, the editorial curve), and a commander entered through its colors, the combos it is a piece of, and tournament lists that 483 of 4,054 commander-eligible cards have at all (the dev branch). Nothing read what the list is about. The friend's reading of the result was right, and it was measured before anything was built (read-only, the dev branch, the real engine in process, `guardDatabaseUrl` first):

  | Precon (commander) | Suggestions' 25 rows, before | The Coach's 15 cheapest cuts, before | Suggestions' 25 rows, after | The 15 cheapest cuts, after |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Sliver Swarm (Sliver Gravemother) | 0 Slivers; 13 rows cost zero mana | 14 Slivers | 11 Slivers; 8 at zero | 6 Slivers |
  | Vampiric Bloodlust (Edgar Markov) | 3 Vampires, each on a combo line; 12 at zero | 11 Vampires | 25 Vampires; 0 at zero | 0 Vampires |
  | Elven Empire (Lathril, Blade of the Elves) | 1 Elf (Llanowar Elves); 9 at zero | 8 Elves | 25 Elves; 0 at zero | 3 Elves |
  | Enduring Enchantments (Anikthea, Hand of Erebos) | 1 enchantment, a land (Urza's Saga); 11 at zero | 12 enchantments | 14 enchantments; 10 at zero | 8 enchantments |

  The Coach ranks only a list over its maximum, so its columns are the precon as if one card over, through the panel's own ranker with the combos route's signals. Before, eight cards were in all four suggestion lists (Lotus Petal, Chrome Mox, Mox Amber, Mox Opal, Mox Diamond, Everflowing Chalice, Urza's Saga, Darksteel Citadel).
- **Why, read from the code.** The candidate pool was the 300 most played cards in the deck's colors plus combo and tournament cards, so the most played Sliver (rank 3,189) was never a candidate. Inside that pool popularity barely separates the cards, so the curve term ordered them, and the template's two 0-mana slots are a 100 % gap in any list without a 0-mana card (LATER row 477, not this build's to fix). In the Coach, global rank is the strongest argument to cut, and a Sliver is played in Sliver decks alone.
- **What a theme is** (`src/lib/games/mtg/themes.ts`, pure and client-safe, declared as `RecommendMeta.themes`). A type word on the type line, read two ways, and the evidence says which. *The list's count*: a type on at least ten of its cards; three everyday words need a share of the nonland cards instead of a count (Artifact 45 %, Enchantment 33 %, Human 35 %), and a counted type most of whose cards carry a bigger theme is that theme's shadow (the ten Druids of an Elf list are Elf Druids). *The commander's rules text naming a type*, read literally and narrowly: reminder text, the card's own name, the types a token is made with and the types an effect turns something into are taken out first, a word after "non-" is no mention, and the name alone is never enough — the commander carries the type itself, or four cards of the list do. Subtypes, and the three card types a list is built around (Artifact, Enchantment, Planeswalker); a land face gives none. A theme has a strength (full at twenty cards; a named one starts at a half) and a selectivity (1, or .3 for the three everyday words).
- **The reading, checked on every precon and every commander before it was wired.** Of the 182 precon rows the reader finds a theme on 97 and none on 85 (Atraxa, Meren, Oloro: lists built around what a type line cannot show, LATER row 480). On the lists built around a type it reads 13 to 43 cards of it, where the 2,000 most played nonland cards would put one or two there by chance. Of the 4,144 commander-eligible cards, 539 read a theme alone (Dragon 26, Hero 25, Elf 24, Villain 24, Spirit 21, Goblin 16). Reading all of them is what found the misreadings the rules above answer: Anikthea's "3/3 black Zombie creature" and Obuun's "X/X Elemental creature" (what an effect turns something into), Taskmaster's "legendary Human Mercenary Villain creature" (a copy effect restating types), Tovolar's "Human Werewolves you control" (an everyday word the commander carries), Squee's own name, Sliver Queen's token.
- **Suggestions** (`engine.ts`, `queries.ts`, `rank.ts`). The engine reads the list's themes first: ONE statement by primary key for the list's own type lines and the commanders' rules text, then the adapter's pure read. The strongest three each bring a pool of the sixty most played cards carrying their word, through the same filter as every other source (legal, in colors, in budget, not in the deck): the fourth candidate source. A candidate carrying a theme scores `THEME_WEIGHT` × strength × selectivity, and its line leads the evidence: "Has the Vampire type, which Edgar Markov's rules text names" with "27 of your cards share it", or "Shares the Sliver type with 41 of your cards", under the source "Your list". The four older weights keep their ratios (each scaled by .75), so a list read for no theme is ranked as before: 85 of 85 such precons, card for card over the first fifty rows. Autofill and Swap Lab call the gather and the ranker without themes and get what they got (LATER row 481).
- **The Cut Coach** (`cuts.ts`, `cut-coach-panel.tsx`). The panel reads the same themes from the same entries on every edit, with no fetch: the type lines and the commander's text are already in the editor. A card that carries one gets a keep line ("Has the Vampire type, which Edgar Markov's rules text names. Cutting it leaves 27 in your list"; "Shares the Sliver type with 40 of your other cards. Cutting it thins that type in your list") and `THEME_PROTECTION` × strength × selectivity comes off its score. The line is a trailing cost, the first of the keep lines, not the lead: a card's cut signals still say why it ranks where it does, and they are what tells one card of a theme from the next. Complete-combo pieces still sort last, whatever else.
- **The yardstick: the precons as the outside judge** (174 distinct lists, the dev branch, two seeds; `scripts/.tmp/fit-yardstick.ts`, untracked). A precon is built around its commander, so two questions have an answer nobody here wrote. *Hold ten nonland cards out of each list: do Suggestions' 25 rows bring them back?*

  | Lists (seed 1; seed 2 in brackets) | Held-out theme cards back, before | after | All held-out cards back, before | after | Rows carrying a theme, of 25 |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 66 built around a subtype (68) | 0.4 % (0.9 %) | 28.3 % (27.8 %) | 2.7 % (3.8 %) | 11.1 % (11.3 %) | 15.9 (15.2) |
  | 23 built around a card type (22) | 4.7 % (5.1 %) | 15.7 % (11.0 %) | 5.2 % (5.9 %) | 10.4 % (7.7 %) | 20.4 (19.8) |
  | 85 read for no theme (84) | none held | none held | 3.1 % (2.4 %) | 3.1 % (2.4 %) | 0 |

  *Slip eight cards from other precons into each list: does the Coach name a stranger before a card that belongs?* (0.5 = it cannot tell.) On the lists with a theme (95 and 96): 0.667 and 0.658 before, 0.762 and 0.752 after, and the strangers among the eight cheapest cuts went from 21.1 % and 19.4 % to 31.1 % and 29.6 %. The lists without a theme are as they were (0.668 and 0.694).
- **The three numbers, and what the yardstick could not say.** `THEME_WEIGHT` .25: .3 brings back 13.2 % and .4 13.6 % on the typal lists, with the theme on 19 and 23 of the 25 rows; .25 takes three quarters of the gain there is and leaves the older signals a third of a typal list. `EVERYDAY_SELECTIVITY` .3: at full weight all 25 rows of an artifact or enchantment list carried the word and none of the other held-out cards came back; at .3 the theme cards came back as often and the others nearly as before. `THEME_PROTECTION` .15: the first build protected at .25 and by strength alone, and the yardstick liked it (0.783), but read on the four lists it had moved the cuts onto the wrong cards: no Sliver among the Sliver precon's fifteen cheapest (its weakest came after a ramp staple), and the enchantment precon's enchantresses fifth and sixth. .15 is what the popularity signal holds against a card at rank 2,000, so a full-strength theme cancels that much and no more. What the precons cannot price: they hold few of the staples the older signals are good at, so what a theme row crowds out does not show (LATER row 478).
- **Verified.** In the browser pane's Chromium at 1440 × 900 against the local dev server: a draft from the Vampire precon shows 25 rows led by "Has the Vampire type, which Edgar Markov's rules text names", sources "Your list, EDHREC, …", and the expansion's "Your list · high · 27 of your cards share it" above the older evidence; the same list one card over (saved as a guest deck on the dev branch, deleted with its own edit key, 204 then 404) ranks 87 cuts with the first Vampire 21st, its keep line amber in the expansion; a draft from the Atraxa precon shows no theme line. No console or server error from the build (the page's only errors are the analytics beacon's CORS refusals on localhost, older). `pnpm smoke:recommend` against the dev server: all green, with three new checks through the draft route (Edgar Markov alone gets Vampires, the list's own line leading; a theme row still carries the older evidence; the Talrand fixture carries no theme line). The theme pool's statement, EXPLAIN ANALYZE on the dev branch: 1.4 ms and 390 buffers for Sliver through `ci_search_gin`, 20 to 30 ms for an everyday word (LATER row 485). A production build compiles; no file under `src/app` changed, so no route did, and there is no migration and no dependency.

### The owned filter sends no list of cards (OWNED-FILTER, 2026-11-24, package 0 of `OWNER-NOTES-2026-11-24-session-prompt.md`, the owner's word at AUTOFILL-OWNED's speed gate: "Rewrite the filter first"; `5b0a038`)

- **Why it exists.** AUTOFILL-OWNED (package 1 of the same file) was to hand Autofill the engine's owned filter, which Suggestions' "Only cards I own" has shipped since P3.7. That filter was a list: the route read every card the player owns, once per request, and the engine sent the list again inside every statement that selects candidates. The package's first step measured it for a collection at the 20,000-row cap, on the dev branch from the owner's machine (150 ms a round trip): 13,565 to 19,995 ids, about 0.9 MB a statement, five statements for Autofill's two gathers, and 4.5 s (median, 13,565 ids) to 6.6 s (19,995 ids) more than the 2.0 to 2.7 s the gathers take unfiltered, against a rule of 300 ms at the median and 1 s at worst. The database's share was 41 to 195 ms; the rest was the list travelling (one megabyte of parameters alone took 1.2 s from that machine). Production's functions run in the database's region, so it should have paid a fraction, which nobody measured. The owner was shown that and chose the rewrite, and the session that ran the step built it.
- **What it is.** The hook takes the player, not their cards: `ownedBy`, a user id, on `CandidateFilter`, `GatherOptions` and `RecommendOptions`, where `ownedCardIds` was a set. The condition (`ownedCondition`, `src/lib/recommend/queries.ts`) is a probe for the row at hand: does a printing of this card sit in this player's collection. One id is bound, the player's. Suggestions' route asks whether the player has a collection (`hasCollection`, one row) and hands the engine the session's user; its answer (`owned.applied`, `owned.reason`) is unchanged, and a guest or a player with none still gets the full list and the reason. `ownedIdentityIds` lost its whole-collection branch, which nothing calls now. Undefined is still "hook off", and a player with no collection row is still an empty pool, never an unfiltered one.
- **Three shapes were measured before one was kept** (a collection at the cap, the dev branch; the plans and the scripts are in the evidence folder).
  - *Build the owned set once inside the condition, fenced so it cannot be flattened* (a MATERIALIZED common table expression inside `IN`). It is still a semi join to the planner. Where the planner expects one outer row it takes a nested loop and rescans the whole set for each row it actually finds: 4,494 rows against 19,995, 65.7 million comparisons, 8.8 s for a theme pool on the word Human. Dropped.
  - *The same subquery where the planner can only hash it* (not a top-level condition). The set is hashed once, about 40 ms, but the planner then took a table scan per row for the legality check: 11 million comparisons, 2.6 s on the same statement. (A reading of the plan, not a proof: with the set's fixed cost added, the two legality plans' estimates differ by 1 %, which the planner treats as a tie.) Dropped.
  - *A probe per row* (a correlated `EXISTS`). Every step is an index lookup (`cp_by_identity`, then the collection's primary key), so however the joins are ordered the cost has a ceiling: a lookup for each row scanned, or the player's rows read once. With the table's statistics fresh, no statement took longer than 0.4 s in any trial (the minute after an import is below). Its weak point was the popularity pool: 0.35 s there, for a collection of 2,500 rows as much as for one at the cap. Kept, with the fix below.
- **Why the popularity pool was slow, and the one line that fixed it.** The pool scans about 24,000 cards for a four-colour deck, and Postgres expected 144. The guess comes from the colour check: it cannot estimate a bit test (`(ci_mask & ~deck) = 0`), so it assumes 0.5 % pass. Expecting 144 rows, it probes the collection once per row it scans. So the popularity pool, and only it, spells the same test as the list of colour identities that fit inside the deck's (`colorFitCondition`, `identitiesWithin`: 32 values at most for Magic), and only while the hook is on. Postgres counts a list of a column's values from the column's statistics (it then expects about 9,500 rows where about 24,000 pass, close enough to choose well), and chooses for itself: start from the player's cards when they are few, walk the pool in order when they are many. With the hook off every statement is the text it was.
- **The same rows.** A scratch script wrote collections of 100, 1,000 and 20,000 random Magic printings for throwaway users and compared each statement, as the engine now sends it, with the same statement carrying the id list by hand the way the old code built it: the popularity pool in three scopes, the tournament candidates, the combo recheck over 400 ids and three theme pools, for five commanders. Ordered statements had to match id for id, the recheck as a set. 259 of 259 checks pass: the same rows in the same order, every candidate of Autofill's two gathers and every card Suggestions answers is owned, and a player with no rows, or an id that is no player, gets an empty pool where the hook-off filter returns 300.
- **What the hook costs now**, in the database's own time (`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE)`, each statement built by the shipped condition builder, once the table's statistics are fresh):

  | Collection | Popularity pool, 4 colours | Land pool | Theme pool (Sliver, Human, Artifact) | Tournament candidates | Combo recheck |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 100 rows | 30 ms less | 2 ms less | +1, then 18 and 20 ms less | 4 ms less | +14 ms |
  | 2,500 rows | +4 to +8 ms | +26 ms | +1, +45, +46 ms | +15 ms | +16 ms |
  | 10,000 rows | +67 to +81 ms | +64 ms | +1, +43, +49 ms | +16 ms | +18 ms |
  | 19,995 rows, drawn at random | +39 to +58 ms | +19 ms | +2, +50, +51 ms | +16 ms | +15 ms |
  | 19,995 rows, the least played cards | +256 to +259 ms | +41 ms | +2, +65, +59 ms | +20 ms | +23 ms |

  A small collection makes the statements faster than no filter at all, because they start from its few cards. The last row is the worst case there is: nothing the player owns is near the top of the pool, so the pool is read to its end. For a request that is about 80 ms at 2,500 rows and about 125 ms at the cap for Autofill (two pools, two tournament statements, the recheck), 360 ms in the worst case; for Suggestions 40 to 170 ms at 2,500 rows and 70 to 220 ms at the cap, by how many of its three theme pools are for everyday words.
- **The rule, on the new code.** Step 0's own run, again: two collections at the cap, five commanders, five runs each, Autofill's two gathers issued together on the one default connection, from the same machine. The hook adds 40 ms at the median for both collections; the worst of 25 run pairs is 377 ms (the cap test's rows) and 210 ms (the 19,995 most played cards). The id list added 4,488 and 6,623 ms at the median. An owned Suggestions request and an owned Autofill request now send statements of 1,100 to 2,100 characters each (`DB_LOG=1`): each binds the player's id once, and the popularity pool the colour identities too.
- **What it costs where the list was cheap.** For a collection of a few thousand cards, in production, the list was a small thing: about 100 KB a statement over a link inside one region, a millisecond or two of planning, and the same execution. The probe is 4 to 46 ms of database work on each statement there. So an ordinary collector's owned Suggestions request does a little more work in the database than before, about a tenth of a second by the dev branch's figures, and sends about half a megabyte less. That is the trade the rewrite makes: nothing travels, the cost no longer depends on where the site runs, and it has a ceiling at the cap.
- **The minute after an import.** Until autovacuum has analyzed the table, Postgres still believes the player's collection is empty, and it starts every statement from the collection. That costs about 6 µs a row, so 15 ms a statement at 2,500 rows and 110 to 155 ms at the cap, and the combo recheck does worse: 90 ms at 2,500 rows and 0.5 to 0.7 s at the cap. An owned request made in that minute by a player at the cap takes about 1.3 s longer than it will a minute later. Nothing is wrong in it and nothing is slow afterwards. Analyzing the table at the end of the import would close the window; the import is outside this package (LATER row 511).
- **Tests.** `pnpm check` green: 3,964 tests in 328 files (3,937 in 327 on `a430816`), lint at the baseline's five warnings. The 27 new ones: the condition's one shape and its one parameter; the colour check's two spellings and that they pass the same identities for every deck (all 32 masks against all 32); which statement counts its colours and when (`queries.test.ts`); the four statements a request can send with the hook, through the real builders over a fake database client (`owned-filter.test.ts`, new); the engine handing the player to every one of them through both entry points (`engine.test.ts`); and the route's five owned cases. Twelve one-line mutations each turn at least one of them red (drop the condition; a probe not keyed by the player; a probe not tied to the row; the colours counted with the hook off, never, or in every statement; the pool forgetting to say it scans; the list of identities missing the deck's own; the gather or Suggestions dropping the player; the route skipping the collection check or applying the hook unasked).
- **Verified against a local production build of this tree.** `pnpm smoke:collection`: 52 of 52, its existing owned checks unedited and one new pair for a collection at the cap (20,000 rows, 13,566 cards: applied, every suggestion an owned card, none already in the deck). `pnpm smoke:recommend`: 69 of 69. `pnpm smoke:autofill`: the baseline's 69 of 71; the two that fail are the dev branch's stale feeds and its empty tournament aggregate for Atraxa (LATER row 489). `pnpm build`: the route table and both stylesheets are identical to `ea6aa27`'s, line for line and hash for hash.
- **Decisions the build made** (the owner can flip any). One shape for the hook, not one per statement. The colour list only in the popularity pool and only with the hook on, so no unfiltered statement changed by a byte. The whole-collection read deleted rather than kept for a caller that does not exist. No change to the import.
- **Not verified.** The filter's cost in production: the costs above are the dev branch's (a compute of 0.25 to 1 CU; production's is 0.25 to 2), and no filtered statement has been timed there (the session's read below ran none, and the owner's try, which did, came with no figure). A table holding many players' collections: the dev branch held one throwaway player at a time, so the planner's estimates for a player who is not among the table's most common values were not seen. The first rewrite's wall-clock for Suggestions from the owner's machine (4 to 6 s a request, 11 statements at two round trips each) is too noisy to show a difference of a tenth of a second, so Suggestions is judged on the statements' own times.
- **Production** (`5b0a038`, pushed 2026-11-25 19:37:32 Z on the owner's "Push all three", under AUTOFILL-OWNED's `5d310c8` and over the record commit `a430816`, the owner told beforehand that the push ships the three; Vercel success 19:38:19 Z and CI run 45687054135 green 19:43:11 Z, both on `5d310c8`). It had been fast-forwarded into local `main` at 13:39:43 Z that day on the owner's "Merge it locally"; asked then whether to push it alone, the owner chose "Wait for package 1". **Read from outside on the owner's OK** (the next section's Production bullet has the whole read): one signed-out request by curl at 19:52:06 Z, `GET /api/decks/[id]/recommendations?owned=1` for the owner's public demo deck. It answered 200 in 0.87 s, marked never to be stored, with `owned: { requested: true, applied: false, reason: "signed-out" }`, 25 suggestions and 13 hidden. That is this build's engine answering on production's data with the hook off. **The filter itself was not read there by a session.** It runs only for a signed-in player who has a collection, which a session cannot be on production. Offered a read-only timing of its statements in the production database or a try of their own, the owner chose "I'll try it signed in" and, asked the same day how the try of both boxes had gone, answered "Both worked" (the next section's Production bullet has what they were given to look for). That is the owner's word, and it came with no figure. The probe's cost in production and its plans on a table of many players' collections are still unmeasured: LATER rows 511 and 512 carry what could go wrong there, and row 517 is done.
- **Left for later:** LATER rows 510 (a card's identity on its collection row, which would make the probe one index read), 511 (the minute after an import), 512 (the bit test's estimate in every other statement) and 517 (the filter on production with a real sign-in: done 2026-11-25, the owner's try).

### Only cards I own, in the Autofill sheet (AUTOFILL-OWNED, 2026-11-25, package 1 of `OWNER-NOTES-2026-11-24-session-prompt.md`, the owner's idea 1 of 2026-11-24: "upload all the cards you have and it builds the best deck from them"; `5d310c8`)

- **Why it exists.** A player could import a collection, pick a bracket in the Autofill sheet, and filter Suggestions by what they own, but Autofill itself never looked at the collection, and a new deck never learned its player had one (LATER row 165). This package joins the parts: the sheet has "Only cards I own", and a signed-in player with an imported collection gets a starter list made of their own cards, inside the bracket they pick. It builds on OWNED-FILTER (the section above): the engine's filter takes the player, so nothing here reads or sends a list of cards.
- **What a player sees.** In the sheet's controls: Budget, Keep my N cards, "Only cards I own", Reroll. Ticked, the list is asked for again once, from the collection. Budget is switched off with its pick still in view, and one line under the controls says why: "Budget is off. Price limits don't apply to cards you own." The notes above the list say what was assumed and what is open, in that order: "Basic lands are added as needed and aren't checked against your collection.", then, if the collection was too small, "Filled 91 of 99. Your collection ran out of cards that fit, leaving 8 slots open in Mana value 1.", then the target's lines. The footer counts where the price estimate was: "70 of these 91 cards are from your collection." Add adds exactly the cards on screen. The deck's header then reads, for example, "You own 88/100 · missing ≈ $25": the missing cards are the basic lands the list assumed, and the commander if the player does not own it.
- **The route** (`POST /api/decks/autofill`). One optional flag, `ownedOnly`; only `true` acts.
  - It is the one ask that reads the session, and it reads it only then, after the cheap 400s and before anything is read for the list. Any other body runs the code it ran before: no session read, no collection read.
  - The answer is a filtered list or no list. Signed out is 401 with one sentence. A player with no collection row is 409 with `reason: "no-collection"`. Neither falls back to an unfiltered list.
  - Both candidate gathers get the player (`ownedBy`, spread in only when set), so every ranked pick is a card they own any printing of. The reach step and the target's ceiling work on those same pools, so they cannot bring in a card the player lacks.
  - A per-card budget in the same body is ignored (decision 5).
  - The answer carries `owned: { applied: true }`, and only then. Its `cards` still holds the commander and the kept cards, which need not be owned.
  - A second limit, keyed by the player, at the address limit's numbers (20 a minute, 200 an hour). The address limit runs first for every ask, as before; the player's is spent once the session is proven, so a signed-out caller cannot use up a player's allowance, and a refusal for "no collection" counts against the player.
  - Caching intent unchanged: dynamic and never stored. With the flag the answer depends on who asks.
- **The words, and where each lives.** All three ride the answer's `notes`, which the sheet prints as sent.
  - *The open slots* are the shared planner's (`shortfallNote`, `src/lib/recommend/autofill.ts`; `finishShell` takes the new case as an option). It counts the slots left open and names the groups that stayed short. It never names a budget.
  - *Building up to the target* is Magic's (`mtgReachNotes`). With the new member of its input it says "Added 2 Game Changers from your collection to show your Bracket 4 target" and "Still 4 Game Changers short of Bracket 4. Autofill had no more to bring in from your collection".
  - *The basic lands* are Magic's too: a new optional line on its Autofill declaration (`base.collectionNote`), which the route sends first, and only when the list holds basic lands the template added.
  - The last two are the additions to the game contract the owner approved as decision 9. The shared code names no card type and no bracket.
- **The sheet asks nothing new.** The editor tells it what it knows of the collection, in one of four words, and the box follows.
  - *Available:* the box can be ticked.
  - *Unavailable* (a guest, or an account with no collection): disabled, with the Suggestions toggle's hint and its link to Account. The two controls share their words through one small file (`src/components/editor/owned-copy.tsx`).
  - *Checking* (a new deck's editor is still asking): disabled, no line.
  - *Failed:* disabled, "Couldn't check your collection."
  - The tick is the sheet's own, like Budget: nothing stores it on the deck (decision 6), and a reopened sheet starts unticked.
  - The route's two refusals each get a sentence and Retry, and nothing is asked again until the player acts. The box stays ticked, so the sheet never asks for an unfiltered list on the player's behalf.
- **A new deck learns the collection.** A saved deck is told by its own load. A new deck has no load, so it reads the sign-in state the header already holds and, for a signed-in player, asks `GET /api/collection` once when the editor opens. A guest's new deck asks nothing. From then on a new deck behaves as a saved one with a collection: cards are marked as owned, the "You own N/100" line shows, and the price estimate and the Fit sheet count owned cards at $0 (the first half of LATER row 165, closed). Add also hands the editor the picks that came from the collection, so the line is right in the same render as the list; the usual lookup then confirms it and covers the basic lands.
- **Suggestions on a new deck** (decision 8). Its own "Only cards I own" stays off until the deck is saved and says so: "Filtering by your collection starts once the deck is saved." The route a new deck's Suggestions use takes no such flag, so without this gate the toggle would have ticked and filtered nothing. The first save, which any Add makes, enables it with no reload.
- **The privacy page** carries the sentence the owner approved as decision 7: "The collection is private. It is only ever shown to you, and used to mark the cards you own and to build lists for you, and you can wipe it from the account page at any time."
- **Tests.** `pnpm check` green: 3,999 tests in 328 files (3,964 at `5b0a038`), lint at the baseline's five warnings. The 35 new ones:
  - the route (9): nothing about the player is read without the flag and both gathers get the options they always got; 401; 409; a refused body is a 400 before the session is read; the player's limit; a collector's ask; no line about basic lands when the list holds none; a collection of thirty cards at a target of 4; Game Changers the collection holds come in.
  - the planner (2), Magic's notes and declaration (2), the limiter (1).
  - the sheet (13): one more ask per tick, with the flag and without the per-card budget, and the unticked body byte for byte; Budget off and its reason; each of the four states; the footer's count; both refusals; what Add hands over.
  - the Suggestions panel (2) and the editor (6): a guest's new deck sends no request about a collection; a collector's new deck reads it once, also under a double mount; the ownership line and the owned marks show with the list after Add.
  - The three pins the brief names are untouched: the planner's fixed-seed golden, the route's "no-goals plan exactly" and the sheet's request-body pins.
  - 36 one-line mutations each turn at least one test red. The first run caught 34; the two that survived (the flag sent after the collection stops being available; the collection read twice on a double mount) each got a test.
- **Verified against a local production build of this tree**, on the dev branch.
  - `pnpm smoke:collection`: 62 of 62 (52 before). The ten new checks sign in a fresh test player whose collection is a commander and the thirty most played cards in its colour that are not lands or Game Changers, and ask at a target of 4: the list is exactly those thirty and 37 Islands, the notes are the three above, a signed-out ask gets 401 and a player with no collection 409.
  - `pnpm smoke:autofill`: the baseline's 69 of 71 (LATER row 489 has the two).
  - `pnpm build`: the route table is `5b0a038`'s, line for line. The main stylesheet gained one rule, 23 bytes (the dimmed thumb of a switched-off Budget); the other is unchanged.
  - The unticked path, two ways. One fixed-seed ask (Atraxa, seed 1,234,567) answers the same 97,065 bytes on both builds. Through the route's own handler with the statement log on, both send the same 12 statements, and none names a session, a user or a collection.
  - On `/decks/new` both builds make one request for the sign-in state. The editor reading it adds none.
- **Read in the browser pane**, on a throwaway local page that drew the real editor with a stand-in sign-in and collection (the pane holds no session; the page was deleted before the check).
  - The walk-through: the sheet opens, the box enables once the collection answers, a tick reloads the list, Add saves, and the header read "You own 88/100 · missing ≈ $25" at the first reading, 164 ms after the press and before any lookup, with 88 rows marked.
  - Layout at 320, 390, 768 and 1280 px in both themes: the controls wrap without pushing the page sideways, and the new lines are 6.7 to 1 or better against their background. (At 320 px with a two-artist commander the page itself is 62 px too wide, which is the editor's art credit: LATER row 469, older than this build.)
  - Every state of the box, and both refusals.
- **Decisions the build made** (the owner can flip any).
  1. *The open-slots sentence* is "…, leaving 8 slots open in Mana value 1." where the plan's example read "…; Mana value 1 was left short." It states the number of open slots, and it reads right for one group or several.
  2. *The still-short sentence* keeps "Autofill had no more to bring in" and adds "from your collection". "Your collection had no more" would be untrue when the collection holds more Game Changers and the kept cards leave no pick to swap out.
  3. *The basic-lands line* is sent only when the list holds basic lands the template added, and comes first.
  4. *One line under the controls* serves the box: why it is off, or, ticked, why Budget is. It sits under the row, so no control moves when it appears.
  5. *A refusal keeps the tick.* Unticking for the player would ask for an unfiltered list they did not ask for.
  6. *Add marks the collection's picks as owned at once.* Without it the line would wait for the owned lookup, which is sent 600 ms after the list changes, and read as if the player owned none of the new cards until it answered.
  7. *An empty list shows no ownership line.* A new deck now knows its player's collection, so a collector's empty new deck would have opened on "You own 0/0". This also changes a saved deck with a collection and no cards, which read "You own 0/0" before.
  8. *The session is read the way every collection read reads it*, through the five-minute cookie cache, not the stricter read the delete routes use.
  9. *"you" keeps its emphasis* in the privacy sentence, as the old sentence had it. The words are the approved ones.
- **Not verified.**
  - The list from a collection, on production, by a session. A guest's view was read there (the Production bullet below); the list itself needs a signed-in player who has a collection, so the try was the owner's, who reported that both boxes worked. No session has seen it.
  - A real signed-in browser. The pane cannot hold a sign-in, so the signed-in screens were read with stand-in answers, and the real route was exercised by the smoke, which signs a test player in inside the script.
  - A real collection. The fixtures are thirty cards and a stand-in; step 0's sample collections are the only measure of how full a list gets.
  - Speed in production. From the owner's machine the smoke's owned ask took 6.9 s against the dev branch, where each statement is two round trips of 150 ms. The owner's try on production came with no timing.
  - Safari and Firefox.
- **One line of the brief does not hold: the checkbox's touch target is 42 px, not 44.** It is the shared Checkbox's, the same on every checkbox of the site since U10a: its hit area is placed from inside the box's 1 px border. The new box is no different from the "Keep my N cards" box beside it, and its label is a target too. Not changed here (LATER row 513).
- **Production** (`5d310c8`, pushed 2026-11-25 19:37:32 Z on the owner's "Push all three", with OWNED-FILTER's `5b0a038` and the record commit `a430816` under it, the owner told beforehand that the push ships the three, and `git log origin/main..main` naming those three; Vercel success 19:38:19 Z and CI run 45687054135 green 19:43:11 Z, its `pnpm check` the authoritative one). It had been fast-forwarded into local `main` at 19:37:27 Z on the same word. Nothing had landed on `main` or `origin/main` since the build's own check, so the tree pushed is the tree that was checked, built and smoked above. **Read from outside on the owner's OK in the question dialog** ("Yes, read as a guest"; 2026-11-25 19:51:58 to 19:53:19 Z; `origin/main` was `5d310c8` throughout, so its deploy is what was read): two signed-out requests by curl and two page views in the browser pane, as the owner had been told. The pane was signed out (its header read "Sign in") and not on screen, so the pages were read by their text and their requests, not as pixels. Cloudflare's beacon was not blocked, so the two views are in its count.
  - *The privacy page* carries the approved sentence, with "you" emphasised. That is acceptance line 15, the one line that waited for production.
  - *An owned ask, signed out:* `POST /api/decks/autofill` with `ownedOnly: true` answered 401 in 1.06 s with "Sign in to build a list from your collection" and no list, marked never to be stored. The build before this one would have ignored the flag and answered with a list, so this is the new route serving.
  - *Suggestions with `owned=1`, signed out,* for the owner's public demo deck: 200 in 0.87 s, the full list of 25 with the reason `signed-out`, as it answered before OWNED-FILTER (the section above).
  - *A guest's new deck* (`/decks/new` with Atraxa as its commander and the sheet open): the box is in the controls after Budget, unticked and disabled, and described by its line, "Sign in and import a collection on your Account page to filter by cards you own", whose link goes to `/account`. Budget's three choices are enabled with All picked. The list loaded as it always has: "Add 99 cards", "Est. total $764 (cheapest printings)", and "With these added, the cards show at least Bracket 4 (Optimized)."
  - *That page's requests,* by its resource timing and the pane's network log: six to the site's API (the sign-in state once, the commander's seed and its art, the combos read twice, and one Autofill ask, 200), none about a collection, and no deck write. Add was not pressed and nothing was edited, so no deck was created. The only rows these reads wrote are the rate-limit counters any visitor's request writes.
  - **Not read there:** the ticked box and everything after it (the list from a collection, its notes and footer, Budget switched off, Add and the ownership line), the 409, the player's limit, the two refusals' sentences in the sheet, Suggestions' toggle on a new deck, the light theme, any measured width, Safari, Firefox and a phone. Most of these need a signed-in player who has a collection. Asked how the two "Only cards I own" boxes should be checked on production, the owner chose "I'll try it signed in" over a read-only timing in the production database, so that try was the owner's: the next line.
  - **The owner's signed-in try** (2026-11-25, after the read above, on production with their own account and collection). They had been given what to look for. In the sheet on a new Commander deck: the box becoming tickable a moment after the page loads and, ticked, the list reloading once, Budget greyed out with its line, the basic-lands note first and the footer's count. After Add: the ownership line at once, with the owned cards marked. On that saved deck: Suggestions' own box leaving only cards they own. And to say so if either list took noticeably longer than with the box unticked, or showed a card they do not own other than basic lands. Asked how the try of both boxes had gone, the owner answered "Both worked". That is their word, and no figure came with it: which commander, how large the collection, how full the list and how long it took are not recorded, and no session saw the screens. LATER row 517 is done. Still unread by anyone: the 409, the player's limit and the two refusals' sentences on production, and the probe's cost there (LATER rows 511 and 512).
- **Left for later:** LATER rows 501 to 503 (the choice is not saved; assumed basic lands read as missing; unranked owned cards), 513 (the checkbox's touch target), 514 (a list short everywhere names every mana value), 515 (the editor learns the collection once), 516 (a wrong line in the Autofill smoke's header), 517 (both owned filters on production with a real sign-in: done 2026-11-25, the owner's try), and rows 66, 165, 191 and 349, each annotated.

### The collection page (COLLECTION-PAGE, 2026-11-25, package 2 of `OWNER-NOTES-2026-11-24-session-prompt.md`, the owner's idea 1 of 2026-11-24: "a separate page to view your collection of cards and have features to build off that"; `c08a9e9`)

- **Why it exists.** A player could import a collection and, since AUTOFILL-OWNED (the section above), build a starter list from it, but only by first finding a commander some other way. Nothing showed the collection itself: Account has its counts, its import and its delete. This package is the page the owner asked for, in the shape they chose at planning ("I pick from ones I own"): the commanders in the collection, each with how much of the collection fits it, one press from a deck built from those cards, and the collection as a list behind them.
- **What a player sees.** `/collection`, opened from the account menu's "Collection" or from "View your collection" beside Account's import.
  - *Signed out:* the two sign-in buttons under one sentence, and nothing about any collection. Signing in comes back to this page (through Account, where the deck claim runs).
  - *Nothing imported:* "No collection imported yet" and a button to Account's import.
  - *Commanders you own* (the page opens here): sixty a page. A row is the commander's name (a link to its card page), its colors, "412 of your cards are in its colors", and "Build from my collection". One line above the list says how many commanders are among how many cards, and that commanders with more colors come first because more of the player's cards fit them. A banned or not-legal commander is named in one quiet line under the list and has no build action anywhere on the page.
  - *Your cards:* one row per card with its copies added up over every printing and finish, sixty a page, a name filter, and two orders (A to Z, or EDHREC rank). A row is one link to the card's page.
  - Every signed-in state carries a link to Account's import ("Update or delete your collection", or the empty state's button).
- **The measurement came first** (the plan's rule: the reads together under 500 ms at the cap, or the owner sees the figures with a smaller page size). Three collections of 19,995 rows on the dev branch (Magic printings drawn at random, 13,239 cards; one printing each of 19,995 cards; every commander there is, 4,144, filled up with random cards), each with stale statistics (the minute after an import) and after `ANALYZE`. Each read was run once through the function the page calls, and the statement it sent was then run under `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` three times; the figures are the database's own execution time, the median of three, with planning added in the totals.

  | Read | Database time at the cap | What it returns |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | 1. The player's cards counted by color identity | 83 to 108 ms | at most 32 rows |
  | 2. The owned commanders with their ruling | 94 to 144 ms | every owned commander: 1,742 to 4,144 rows |
  | 3. One page of cards (first page, last page, by name, by rank, four name filters) | 57 to 176 ms | 60 rows and the total |
  | All three together | 316 to 418 ms | |

  So sixty a page stands. No view runs all three: the commanders view runs reads 1 and 2 (182 to 253 ms), the card list runs read 3 alone. Three things the measurement taught:
  - *The page size is not what the reads cost.* Nothing indexes a player's cards by name or by rank, so read 3 gathers and sorts the whole collection before it cuts a page: the last page costs what the first does, and a smaller page would save nothing.
  - *Read 1 was rewritten on its plan.* As first written it joined every collection row to its card and counted distinct cards: 117 to 184 ms, and slower with fresh statistics than with stale ones (Postgres chose a parallel hash join over the printings table). Folding the rows to cards first, then reading each card once, is 83 to 108 ms in every state.
  - *The first two runs timed the wrong statement for read 2.* The script replayed each statement with the parameters postgres.js had logged, and postgres.js rewrites that array in place when it sends it: `true` becomes the text "t", and a replayed "t" is serialized as a boolean again, which makes it false. The replay therefore asked for the cards that are not commanders (11,497 rows where the call had returned 1,742). The plan itself gave it away. The script now copies the parameters and refuses a replay whose row count differs from the call's; the figures above are that third run's.
- **The count beside a commander** is arithmetic on reads 1 and 2, in code (`rankOwnedLeaders`, `src/lib/collection/leaders.ts`): the sum of read 1's rows whose color identity fits inside the commander's, less one for the commander itself. A five-color commander's count is therefore every other card the player owns. A partner is counted on its own colors, and a double-faced commander is one card and one row. The order is that count, then EDHREC rank (unranked last), then the name. What counts is the owner's decision 12 as written: every owned card that fits, lands included, each card once however many printings or copies of it there are.
- **Build from my collection.** The button is the new-deck link with the commander and two one-time flags: the one the hubs' "Start with a starter shell" already uses, which opens the Autofill sheet, and a new one, which ticks "Only cards I own" in that sheet. Both leave the address when the page opens, so a reload reopens nothing.
  - The tick is still the sheet's own state. The editor hands the flag to the sheet the link opened and to no later one: it is spent when that sheet closes, so the Autofill door or the More menu opens an unticked sheet afterwards, and nothing is stored on the deck (decision 6).
  - *One opening request.* A new deck's editor is still asking whether its player has a collection when the sheet opens. A sheet that opens ticked asks nothing while that is unknown: it shows the list's skeleton with the box ticked and not yet pressable. When the editor answers yes, the one request goes out with the flag.
  - *Never an error.* When the editor answers that there is no collection to build from (a guest, an account without one) or that it could not check, the tick is dropped: the one request carries no flag, the list is the ordinary one, and the box reads unticked with the line it always shows in that state. Nothing on this path can reach the route's two refusals.
  - A tick now lives only while there is a collection. That is one rule for both ways a box gets ticked, and it changes one corner of AUTOFILL-OWNED: a box the player ticked, on a deck whose collection then stops being available, used to come back ticked if the collection returned. It now comes back unticked.
- **Shared code.** The page knows one thing about games: which one a collection holds (`COLLECTION_GAME`; the import resolves against the same game). The commander's noun, the format whose rulings decide what can be built, the rank's words and its source's name, the commander index it points to when the collection holds none, and whether a starter list exists at all come off that game's adapter; a test renders the page with an adapter that says "Captain", "Tabletop" and "Zeta" and finds none of Magic's words. No change to the game contract (`src/lib/games/types.ts` is untouched).
- **Private.** The page takes no player from its address: every read is keyed by the session's own user, and a test and the smoke both ask for it with another player's id in the query and get the asker's own page. Signed out it reads nothing. It is rendered for every request and never stored, marked not to be indexed, and disallowed in `robots.txt` beside Account. The build link names a commander and nothing else.
- **The rest.** The account menu's "Collection" opens the page (decision 11; it opened Account's import since Wave 2). Account's import section shows "View your collection" once a collection exists. The build plan's sentence that the collection "schema is ready from M1" says what happened (it was first written in P3.7). No migration, table, index or API route.
- **Tests.** `pnpm check` green: 4,094 tests in 332 files (3,999 in 328 at `7431ec0`), lint at the baseline's five warnings. The 95 new ones:
  - the address (14): what the page reads off a query, the links it builds, and that the two agree; the build link.
  - the commanders' arithmetic and the two statements through the real query builders (12), and the card list's statement (10): each is keyed by the player's id once and carries no list of cards.
  - the page (36), with its reads stood in: who sees what, what each view asks of the database, the order and the counts for the plan's collection, the build links, the banned commander, the fold of a long not-legal line, paging, the filter and both orders, every empty state, and the adapter with other words.
  - the sheet (9), the editor (8) and the new-deck page (6): the ticked open in each state of the collection check, one request each time; the link opened by a guest; the tick spent with its sheet; the hubs' plain starter-shell link unchanged.
  - the header's two pins and the robots pin, updated.
  - 57 one-line mutations: 53 caught on the first run. Three of the four that survived each got a test (a failed check that kept the tick, a plain starter-shell link that opened ticked for a collector, the flags arriving on a page already open). The fourth is not a gap: the new-deck page sets each one-time flag twice, as it does its three older ones, and removing either line changes nothing.
- **Verified against a local production build of this tree**, on the dev branch.
  - `pnpm smoke:collection`: 93 of 93 (62 before), twice: on the build before the records were written and on the final tree's. The 31 new checks write the plan's collection for a fresh test player (a five-color commander, a white one, a red one, a Partner, a double-faced one, a banned one and forty other cards, one of them in two printings) and read the real pages: signed out, the HTML holds no card name; a player with no collection gets the empty state and none of the other player's cards, whatever the address names; the commanders come in the order and with the counts worked out in the script from the fixture alone; the five-color one reads 45 of 46; the banned one is named and has no link; every legal one has its build link, and the owned Autofill for the five-color commander returns only cards of this collection and basic lands; "Your cards" is 46 rows in the database's own A-to-Z and rank orders, with Sol Ring's two printings added up to five copies; and at the 20,000-row cap the card list pages sixty at a time with no row on two pages, a last page and nothing past it.
  - `pnpm build`: the route table gains exactly one line, `/collection`, dynamic; `/decks/new` stays static. The main stylesheet grew by 710 bytes (170,952 to 171,662); the other is unchanged, and the records added nothing to either (the same two files, hash for hash, before and after they were written). The page answers `private, no-cache, no-store`.
  - The build link as a guest, in the browser on that build: both flags left the address, the page made one Autofill request with no flag and none about a collection, the box read unticked with its line, and a reload of what was left opened no sheet and asked Autofill nothing.
- **Read in the browser pane.** The pane holds no sign-in, so the signed-in page was read on a throwaway copy of itself that takes its player from the address (throwaway players with collections of 46, 2,500 and 19,995 rows on the dev branch; the copy, the players and the dev cache were deleted before the check).
  - At 320, 390, 768 and 1280 px in both themes, for both views: no sideways scroll, no name cut off, and under 768 px every control at least 44 px tall. The quiet text is 8.5 to 1 against the dark page and 6.1 to 1 against the light one.
  - Two things the read changed. Long names (a double-faced commander's is two names) were cut with an ellipsis at 320 px and now wrap. And the not-legal line ran to 1,376 px at 320 px for the random 19,995-row collection, which holds 126 such commanders: past ten names the line now counts them and the names open under it.
  - The build flow in the real editor, with a stand-in sign-in and collection: with the collection check held for six seconds the sheet stood ticked and asked nothing, then sent one request with the flag; as a guest, with no collection and with a failed check it sent the plain request and showed no error.
- **Decisions the build made** (the owner can flip any).
  1. *"Colors", not "colours".* The plan's example read "412 of your cards are in its colours"; the site writes "colors" everywhere else.
  2. *The count is literal.* Every card of the game in the collection counts, whatever a deck could do with it: a token, a banned card, another commander. Decision 12 says every owned card, and its other reading ("only cards Autofill can use") is the flip the plan names (LATER row 519).
  3. *A commander's name leads to its card page*, not its hub: the shared page has no hub address to build, and the card page links to the hub.
  4. *The card list's filter is the site's own matcher* in the reading the commander index uses: every typed word somewhere in the name, and near misses from four characters on (so "urzas saga" finds Urza's Saga). The order stays the player's, so a near miss sits where its name or rank puts it.
  5. *The not-legal line folds past ten names*, and names each one with its ruling.
  6. *A card row is one link*; a commander row has two (the name, the build button).
  7. *The link's tick shows while the sheet waits*, and is dropped if the answer is no. Showing it unticked first would have made the box change under the player's eyes in the usual case.
  8. *The page lists one game* (the one the import takes). A collection row of another game, which the import cannot write, would be left out.
- **Where the build differs from the plan, or falls short of it.**
  - *A link opened by somebody else who has a collection opens ticked, for their own cards.* The plan says the sheet opens unticked "by someone the link was sent to". That holds for a guest and for a player without a collection. A signed-in collector who follows the link gets a list from their own collection: the link carries no player, so the page cannot tell them from its sender, and nothing of the sender's is in it (LATER row 520).
  - *The commanders read returns every owned commander, not a page of them.* The count and the order are worked out in code, as the plan has it, so the whole list travels: 304 KB for the random collection, 725 KB when every commander is owned (as JSON; an ordinary collection of 2,500 rows holds a few hundred commanders). The database spends 94 to 144 ms on it. From the owner's machine, 150 ms from the dev branch, that transfer took 0.8 to 4.1 s in the measurement, and the commanders view at the cap took 2.9 s and 1.8 s in the smoke's two runs. Production's functions run beside the database, where it should be a few milliseconds; nobody has measured it there (LATER row 518).
- **Not verified.**
  - The signed-in page on production, by a session. A guest's view was read there (the Production bullet below). The page for a player who has a collection, and "Build from my collection" for a collector, need a real sign-in, which a session cannot have there, so the walk-through (Collection from the account menu, the commanders, "Build from my collection") was the owner's, who reported that it worked. No session has seen it.
  - The reads' cost in production, and their plans on a table that holds many players' collections. The dev branch held one test player at a time, and the owner's walk-through on production came with no timing.
  - A real collection. The fixtures are random printings and hand-picked cards.
  - A real signed-in browser. The signed-in page was read through the throwaway copy, and the real route by the smoke, which signs its test players in inside the script.
  - `pnpm smoke:autofill` was not run: this package changes no route and nothing the planner reads.
  - Safari and Firefox.
- **Production** (`c08a9e9`, pushed 2026-11-25 21:18:28 Z on the owner's "Merge and push", with the two docs commits `f99c662` and `7431ec0` under it, the owner told beforehand that the push carries the three, and `git log origin/main..main` naming those three; Vercel success 21:19:18 Z and CI run 45703130195 green 21:24:25 Z, its `pnpm check` the authoritative one). Local `main` was fast-forwarded to it just before the push. Nothing had landed on `main` or `origin/main` since the build's own check, so the tree pushed is the tree that was checked, built and smoked above. **Read from outside on the owner's OK in the question dialog** ("Yes, read as a guest"; 21:19:57 to 21:20:17 Z; `origin/main` was `c08a9e9` throughout, so its deploy is what was read): two signed-out requests by curl and one page view in the browser pane, as the owner had been told. Nothing was retried. The pane was signed out (its header read "Sign in"). Cloudflare's beacon was not blocked, so the one view is in its count.
  - *`/collection`, signed out:* 200 in 1.32 s from the new route (the build before this one had none), marked never to be stored and not to be indexed, with "Your collection" over the two sign-in buttons, and no list, no build link and no card.
  - *`robots.txt`:* `/collection` is disallowed, after `/account`.
  - *A guest's "Build from my collection" link* (a new deck with Krenko, Mob Boss): both one-time flags left the address when the page opened. The sheet opened with "Only cards I own" unticked and disabled, described by its line, whose link goes to `/account`. The list loaded as any guest's does ("Add 99 cards", "Est. total $1232 (cheapest printings)"), with no error and no Retry.
  - *That page's requests,* by its resource timing and the pane's network log: six to the site's API (the sign-in state once, the commander's seed and its art, the combos read twice, and one Autofill ask, 200), none about a collection, and no deck write. Add was not pressed, so no deck was created. The only rows these reads wrote are the rate-limit counters any visitor's request writes.
  - **Not read there:** anything signed in. That is the page for a player who has a collection ("Commanders you own" and its counts, "Your cards" with its filter, orders and pages), "Build from my collection" for a collector (the box ticked, the one request with the flag, the list from the collection), the account menu's "Collection" item, Account's "View your collection", and how long the page takes with a real collection (LATER row 518). Also unread: the light theme, any measured width, Safari, Firefox and a phone. Asked how the signed-in page should be checked, the owner chose "I'll walk through it" over a walk-through with a read-only timing in the production database, so that walk-through was the owner's (the next line), and the timing is not approved.
  - **The owner's signed-in walk-through** (2026-11-25, after the read above, on production with their own account and collection). They had been given what to look for. From the account menu: "Collection" opening this page and not Account. On "Commanders you own": the commanders with the most colors first, each with its count, and a banned one only in the quiet line under the list. On "Your cards": the count Account shows, a name filtering the list, EDHREC rank re-ordering it, and Next and Previous paging it. On "Build from my collection": a new deck with that commander, the Autofill sheet open, "Only cards I own" ticked a moment later, and one list whose footer counts the cards from the collection; then a reload of that page reopening nothing. On Account: "View your collection" beside the import's heading. And to say so if a commander was missing or miscounted, the box was not ticked, or the page took noticeably long. Asked how the walk-through had gone, the owner answered "It worked". That is their word, and no figure came with it: how large the collection is, how many commanders it holds, which one they built from and how long the page took are not recorded, and no session saw the screens. So the page and its build link work on production for a real collection by the owner's account, and how long the page's reads take there is still unmeasured (LATER row 518).
- **Left for later:** LATER rows 518 (the commanders read sends every owned commander; the page's reads are unmeasured on production), 519 (what the count beside a commander includes), 520 (a build link opened by another collector), 521 (the signed-in page on production: done 2026-11-25, the owner's walk-through), and rows 12 and 504, each annotated.

### A build button where a commander is found (COMMANDER-DOOR, 2026-11-25, package 3 of `OWNER-NOTES-2026-11-24-session-prompt.md`, the owner's idea 4 of 2026-11-24: "a small button from the search result to add commander and then take you to the build page also when you land on the card page there should be a button"; `ccc77ba`, then `31e620f`)

- **Why it exists.** Every commander's hub has carried "Build with this commander" since September, hidden for a banned or not-legal one. Getting there from a search took three presses: the suggestion, the card page's hub link, then the button. The owner asked for the action where a commander is found: on its card page, in the header's search and in the search results. The brief is `COMMANDER-DOOR-session-prompt.md`.
- **What a visitor sees.**
  - *The card page:* a card that can lead a deck has "Build with this commander" (One Piece: "Build with this leader") in a row of its own, directly under the Legality chips and above the printings. It opens a new deck with that card in place ("1 / 100"), and nothing is saved until the first edit. One button: "Start with a starter shell" stays on the hub (decision 14). A player who came from a saved deck's "Browse commanders" also sees "Use for “<deck>”" before it, as on the hub.
  - *The header's search:* when the top suggestion is a legal commander, one more entry sits directly under it, "Build with Atraxa, Praetors' Voice", with a hammer where a row has its thumbnail. It is an option of the list: two presses of the down arrow and Enter open the new deck, and the box keeps its text. Only the top suggestion gets one (LATER row 506).
  - *The search results:* each legal commander's tile on `/cards` has the same sentence as a link under its picture.
  - *One Piece's words carry the card number* in the dropdown and under tiles, "Build with Monkey.D.Luffy (OP01-003)": 97 of its 142 leaders share a name with another, and 17 are Monkey.D.Luffy. This amends decision 13's words for that game.
  - *No action:* Sol Ring (the chip "Commander: legal" means playable, not able to lead); a banned or not-legal commander (decision 15); a token; a Background that is no creature; a removed card, on its card page; and, in search, any card while the deck editor or the new-deck game picker is open, or while a commander pick is pending for that game.
- **One rule, written once.** A card gets a build action when it is a leader candidate and its status in the format a new deck opens in is legal. Its status is that of its current unconditional row there, and legal when it has none; a conditional row (One Piece's pair bans) never blocks. `src/lib/decks/leader-build.ts` holds it: `leaderFormat(game)`, `leaderCanBuild(isLeaderCandidate, status)` and `unconditionalStatus(legality)`. No surface reads a format's default, so "no row" is legal only while the seeded formats default to legal; the helper's test pins that, and that the format is each adapter's first. One table of cases (`leader-build-fixtures.ts`) feeds the tests of the helper, the card page and the grid. The rule has two older homes, each in its own code and left as they are: the commander hub's page (`status === "legal"` over `loadLeaderStatus`) and the collection page's read (`loadOwnedLeaders` with `rankOwnedLeaders`); LATER row 526 holds the three. The action's address and words are a module with no import of its own (`leader-build-link.ts`), because the header ships to every page.
- **Step 0, before any code** (the dev branch, whose Scryfall data is of 2026-10-24).

  | | Magic | One Piece |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Leader candidates | 4,174 (4,144 live, 30 removed) | 142, all live |
  | Banned, current and unconditional | 8 | 2 (OP03-040, ST10-001) |
  | Not legal | 575 live, and the 30 removed | 0 |
  | Restricted | 0 | 0 |
  | A conditional row only | 0 | 1 (OP11-040, a pair ban) |
  | Previews | 90, none with a legality row | 0 |
  | Sharing a name | not counted | 97 of 142, across 32 names |

  No leader candidate is restricted, and every file, export, prop, statement and test pin the brief named was there, so neither of step 0's stops fired. Two cards with a Background type line carry the leader flag: Faceless One, a creature, which gets the button, and Wizard from Beyond, which is not legal. What the editor says about a preview commander: nothing. The validator's "preview card(s): not legal until release" needs a not-legal row, and the ingest writes no row for a preview; a new deck seeded with Magik, Illyana Rasputin (released 2027-04-09) showed no warning.
- **The card page.** The hub's own button island with no starter-shell button, in the wrapper the hubs use. The status is read off what the page's one lookup already loaded, for the `formats` row of the helper's format: no such row, no button; that row with no ruling for the card, legal. No statement and no session read was added, and the caching intent is unchanged: dynamic, kept by the CDN, the same for every visitor. Read on 18 real card pages of the dev branch. The button: Atraxa, Asmoranomardicadaistinaculdacar, Esika (double-faced), Thrasios (a partner; one commander is placed), Magik and Karazikar (previews), Faceless One, OP01-003 and OP11-040. None: Sol Ring, Golos (banned), Whtz (not legal), A-Acererak (removed), Raised by Giants, Wizard from Beyond, a Treasure token, OP03-040 and ST10-001.
- **The dropdown's data.** Each of the three suggest statements selects one more column, last: whether the card has a current unconditional ruling other than legal in the game's format (`buildBlockedSql`, exported from `src/lib/search/suggest.ts`). It is a column, never a condition, so a banned commander's row stays in the answer. A row carries `buildBlocked: true`, a tenth key, only when it is a leader candidate and the column is true; every other row is its nine keys. This amends the row shape WAVE3.md's D2 records.
  - *Its cost,* measured before it was kept (the brief's bar: 5 ms at the median). `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE)` of each statement as `main` builds it and as this tree does, five runs each, interleaved, the whole thing run twice:

    | Text | Statement | Added at the median, planning and execution |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | atraxa | name pass | 1.4 ms |
    | golos | name pass | 1.6 ms |
    | sol | name pass | 1.4 to 1.8 ms |
    | op01-02 (a One Piece card number) | card-number pass | 0.05 to 0.13 ms |
    | urzas saga | name pass, then near misses | under 0.3 ms each |
    | luffy (One Piece) | name pass | under 0.4 ms |

    A Magic name pass pays about 1.2 ms of that in execution, because Postgres answers the column from one hash of the format's 4,348 current rows (a sequential read of that small table) and does not probe the unique index for each of the at most eight rows. It is inside the bar, and it grows with that table (LATER row 528). The grid's one more statement, the legality read over a 60-id page, takes 0.16 to 0.33 ms in the database.
  - *Against the rule on the real data.* A script selected the exported column for every card and compared it with the helper, fed the same card's rows through the grid's own read. Magic: 4,174 of 4,174 leader candidates agree, the column true for 613 (8 banned, 605 not legal); 36,718 of 36,718 cards. One Piece: 142 of 142, true for 2; 2,785 of 2,785 cards; OP11-040, whose only row is conditional, reads false.
  - *A trap that run met.* In the select list of a statement with no join, drizzle writes that list's own columns without their table, so the subquery's "id" was the ruling's and Postgres refused the comparison (`uuid = bigint`). The served statements all join the printing and were never at risk. The column now nests the card's id one level, and a test pins the statement with no join.
- **The entry.** `NameSuggest` takes an opt-in prop that names the games that may get an entry and, per game, whether its words carry the row's printed id. The box reads it for the game whose rows show, so after a fallback the entry is the other game's: typed from a Magic page, "op01-003" offers "Build with Monkey.D.Luffy (OP01-003)" and opens a One Piece deck. The entry is its own kind of list item with a key of its own; its words wrap and are never cut off; the status line still counts suggestions only. The five other boxes pass no prop and are unchanged. The popup now holds up to eight rows, the entry and the footer, which amends WAVE3.md's D0 ("at most 8 rows").
  - *What it costs the list.* The rows under the top one sit lower by the entry's height. Typing "sol" and then "solp" at 390 px with touch, the second row went from 102 px to 150 px: 44 px for a one-line entry, 48 px for a two-line one ("Build with Solphim, Mayhem Dominus"), 28 px with a mouse.
  - *An answer that lands under a highlight.* The highlight is a position in the list. Typing clears it; an answer landing does not, and Base UI gives no way to clear it from outside. So if results arrive while the second position is highlighted, the entry is under the highlight and Enter opens the new deck. A test pins it as it is, and the owner chose to keep the entry under the top result (below). LATER row 527.
- **The header.** `HeaderSearch` builds the prop from one constant: Magic without the card number, One Piece with it. A test pins the constant against each adapter's printed-id declaration, because the header must not ship the adapters. It passes nothing on a `/decks` path: the new-deck page reads `?leader=` once per mount, so the link would open no commander there, and the header reads the path alone, so the picker cannot be told from a draft (LATER row 523). It leaves a game out while a commander pick is pending for it, read per game and never by the page's game (LATER row 522). The pick reads null on the server, so the header's HTML is what it was.
- **The grid.** The grid's request, and only it, carries `format=<code>` after its other parameters; its rows are typed as the editor's wire card; a tile whose card passes the rule gets the link, a block of its own under the picture. On touch the link is a 44 px target with no negative margin and a 2 px gap above it, so a tap on the picture's bottom edge still opens the card and no target overlaps another (read at 320 and 390 px). A long word breaks: "Build with Asmoranomardicadaistinaculdacar" is three lines in the right-hand column at 320 px, and nothing scrolls sideways. The strip's request and tiles are unchanged: without `format` every row answers no rulings, which the rule would read as legal (LATER row 524). Tab order is picture, build, next picture: up to 120 stops on a page of 60 commanders, accepted.
  - *The words under a tile,* measured at 138 px (the tile's width at 320 px): two lines for most names (Atraxa, Thrasios, Karazikar), three for Esika's double-faced name, for Asmoranomardicadaistinaculdacar and for "Monkey.D.Luffy (OP01-003)", four for the longest double-faced name. The build first carried a second form that showed "Build" alone and kept the rest for screen readers; the owner chose the whole sentence, and `31e620f` removed the other.
- **A ban's delay.** The CDN keeps all three answers. A new ban can leave the card page's button and the dropdown's entry up for about a day (fresh an hour, served stale for up to a day) and the grid's link for about an hour (five minutes fresh, an hour stale). The editor flags a banned commander at once.
- **Tests.** 4,179 in 334 files after `31e620f` (4,094 in 332 before the package), lint at the baseline's five warnings. Two new files: the helper's test and the card page's, the first test of that page, which also pins that the page never reads the session, the request's headers or its cookies. The suggest route's test gained six cases and the three components' tests a describe each. In the grid's test only the ten exact pins of the grid's request changed (the strip's two did not), and no site-header pin changed. One-line mutations on the final tree: 51, of which 50 are caught by a test and one by the type checker (the entry's case in the list's item-to-text function: the changed line does not compile). The brief's own 19 are among them.
  - *Acceptance 8* (a pending pick) is proved by three checks. The card page's test, rendered after a pick is written: the "Use for" button with that deck's editor address, in both games, and nothing for the other game's pick. The header's test in both directions: with a Magic pick on a Magic page "atraxa" offers no entry and a One Piece answer still offers its own; with a One Piece pick on a Magic page that answer offers none. And the grid's case. The hub button's own test is untouched.
- **Verified against a local production build of this tree** (the dev branch).
  - The route table is the starting commit's, line for line (86 lines).
  - Script weight, taken as every same-origin script file named in the served page, each fetched once, gzipped and summed: `/privacy` 404,613 to 405,550 bytes (+937, the header's share, on every page); `/cards` 438,490 to 439,828 (+1,338); Atraxa's card page 437,166 to 438,313 (+1,147). The adapter registry did not reach the header's scripts.
  - `pnpm smoke:hubs`, `smoke:seo`, `smoke:optcg` and `smoke:games`: 64, 97, 26 and 63 checks, the same checks with the same results as on the starting commit. The hub pages did not move.
  - The suggest answer for "golos" carries the member, last; the rows for "atraxa" are nine keys; OP03-040 carries it. The three cache headers are what they were.
- **Read in the browser pane** at 320, 390, 768 and 1280 px, dark throughout and light once at 390 px: the card page's row (the button is 44 px tall on touch), the dropdown (every option at least 44 px on touch, the popup 288 px wide at 320 px, nothing sideways) and the grid. A real keyboard walk: "atraxa", down twice, Enter, and the new deck opens with Atraxa and "1 / 100 · 99 to go"; its requests were the sign-in state, the commander's seed, the combos read and the art, with no deck write. A preview commander keeps the button: "Keep the button". The words under a tile: "Full sentence". The entry's place: "Under the top result".

The dev branch's preview flags are a month old, so production's count of preview commanders will differ from the 90 here.
- **Production** (2026-11-25). The owner's word, in the build's session: "Merge and push", after being told the push carries `ccc77ba`, `31e620f` and `0d59119`. Local `main` was fast-forwarded to `0d59119` at 23:00:16 Z (the untracked copy of the brief in the shared checkout was first set aside, byte for byte, because the merge brings the tracked one); `git push origin main` went out at 23:00:24 Z (`23afe59..0d59119`); Vercel reported the production deploy at 23:00:56 Z; CI run 45717903048 was green at 23:05:32 Z. The build row comes directly after the Legality chips' list ("Commander: legal") and before "Printings · 13", and holds one anchor, "Build with this commander", to `/decks/new?game=mtg&leader=7e6b9b59-cd68-4e3c-827b-38833c92d6eb`.
