### A deck total in the Autofill sheet (AUTOFILL-TOTAL, 2026-11-25, package 4 of `OWNER-NOTES-2026-11-24-session-prompt.md`, the owner's idea 5 of 2026-11-24: "have a spot to type your total budget to keep the deck in that range or have a drop down of price range people can choose from"; `3658cb6`, on top of `e21abfd`)

- **Why it exists.** Autofill's Budget is a limit on each card. A budget for the whole deck existed only in "Fit my budget", so a budget deck took two passes: Autofill and Add, then Fit my budget and a review of swaps. The Autofill sheet now takes the total itself and plans the list under it. With a total set, building up to a bracket can no longer bring in a $99 card unnoticed (LATER row 474).
- **What a player sees.** In the sheet's controls, on the line under Budget: "Deck total", a box for whole dollars with Set, and five chips: Not set, $50, $100, $150 and $250. A press or a typed total asks for the list once. The footer then reads the whole deck against the total: "Deck total: est. $97 of your $100", which counts the commander, the cards kept and the cards about to be added, and follows the checkboxes. One line under the controls says what Add will do: "Adding these cards saves $100 as this deck's total. Only you see a deck's total." Add saves the total on the deck, and the toast says so: "Deck total set: $100. Undo takes the cards back, not the total." From then on the deck's goals line reads against it and Fit my budget opens on it. A deck that already has a total opens the sheet on it. Where no list can reach the total, a note above the list says how far over the deck is and why.
- **The measurement came first** (the plan's rule: a preset stays only if at least half the lists reach it). A read-only script on the dev branch built lists in process with the route's own pieces, as AUTOFILL-TARGET's step 0 did: the same 57 commander sets, the same five seeds, no kept cards and no per-card budget, at no total and at each of the four, then priced each list as the route prices it (the commander and every pick at their cheapest printings).

  | Deck total | Lists at or under it, of 285 | The whole deck, median | Picks that differ from the same seed's no-total list, median (9 in 10 lists at most) |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | none | n/a | $895.54 (from $522.93 to $4,029.18) | n/a |
  | $50 | 255 (89.5 %) | $49.55 | 48 of 99 (62) |
  | $100 | 280 (98.2 %) | $98.71 | 33 (52) |
  | $150 | 285 | $147.02 | 26 (46) |
  | $250 | 285 | $242.13 | 16 (37) |

  So all four presets pass. A list lands or does not by its commander, never by its seed: 51 of the 57 land at $50 on all five seeds and six on none (Sheoldred, the Apocalypse at $101.42, Mondrak, Glory Dominus at $42.96, Kozilek, Butcher of Truth at $42.57, Ragavan, Nimble Pilferer at $39.01, Avacyn, Angel of Hope at $36.55 and Delney, Streetwise Lookout at $34.27, each by itself most of the total); at $100 only Sheoldred does not. The cheapest list the pools hold costs $16.28 to $118.96 with its commander (median $21.65); the other 99 cards come to about $16 to $18 for a deck with colors and $36 for a colorless one. No list holds a card with no known price, every list is 100 cards, none has a validation error. The two examples the plan asks for: Etali, Primal Storm at seed 424,242 costs $1,040.57 with no total and $97.21 at $100; Sheoldred, the Apocalypse cannot reach $100 at any seed, and her list comes to $118.96.
- **The owner's answers at the gate** (2026-11-25, the question dialog, each put with these figures). Presets: "All four". Decision 16: "Yes, save it". Decision 17 was put with what the plain rule does to a target (the next bullet), and the owner chose "Keep target where it fits" over the plain rule and over the plan's flip, "Target wins". The figures beside that answer were the first cut's, which kept a Bracket 4 target on 54 and 209 lists at $50 and $100; the rule as built keeps it on 71 and 226, and no list lost a target between the two.
- **The fit step** (`src/lib/recommend/autofill.ts`, inside `buildShell`, after the plan and after the build-up; pure, and nothing in it is sampled, so a seed still means one list).
  - While the deck costs more than the total, the priciest pick that has a cheaper stand-in leaves, and the best-ranked cheaper card of its own group comes in: its mana-value group, or the lands. So the size, the curve and the land count hold. A land with no cheaper ranked land gives its slot to a basic, where a basic costs less.
  - The commander and the kept cards never leave. The planner never sees them as picks, so the route hands it their cost. Anything Autofill planned may leave: a measured staple, a combo completion, a card the build-up brought in.
  - A card coming in has a known price and passes the same bracket check as every other pick.
  - It stops at the first list at or under the total. Where there is none, it stops when no pick has a cheaper stand-in left, which is the cheapest list the pools hold.
  - Money is counted in cents. A basic is counted at the dearest basic the adapter could use for the deck's colors, so the fit never counts the lands low; the figure a player reads uses each basic's own price. On the dev branch the five basics cost $0.08 to $0.11, so the two differ by about a dollar at most. (Wastes is $0.72 there and fills only a colorless deck.)
- **A target under a total** (decision 17 as the owner answered it: the total wins, and the target is kept wherever it fits). Measured first, with the plain rule (the priciest pick leaves, whatever it is, and the build-up then brings a card back only where the deck stays under the total): Game Changers are among the dearest cards, so they leave first, and the list stops just under its total with no room left for one.

  | Target, total | Lists at or under the total that still show the target, plain rule | The same, as built | Picks that differ from the no-total list, median: plain, as built |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 3, $50 | 65 of 285 | 245 | 47, 52 |
  | 3, $100 | 216 | 280 | 33, 35 |
  | 3, $150 | 265 | 285 | 24, 26 |
  | 3, $250 | 278 | 285 | 15, 15 |
  | 4, $50 | 0 | 71 | 48, 56 |
  | 4, $100 | 20 | 226 | 33, 54 |
  | 4, $150 | 65 | 274 | 27, 42 |
  | 4, $250 | 149 | 283 | 18, 22 |

  As built, the fit runs three times over.
  - *First with the target kept.* A swap never leaves the list shorter of its target than it was. A card the target needs (without it the list would be shorter) leaves only for a cheaper card that counts and makes the list whole again; everything else gives way first.
  - *Then the total wins.* Where the first run cannot reach the total, the cards the target needs may leave too, priciest first.
  - *Then the build-up again, under the total.* The best-ranked card that counts comes back in wherever the deck can afford it, the rest of the list making room by another run with the target kept. A try that cannot get back inside the total is undone. So the line "Still 3 Game Changers short of Bracket 4. Autofill had no more to bring in under your $100 deck total (Wizards' list)" is said only when every card that counts was tried against the list as it stands.
  - At a target of 3, every list that lands at $100 shows Bracket 3 (280 of 280), and 245 of the 255 that land at $50. The price is the rest of the list: at Bracket 4 and $100, 54 of the 99 picks differ from the no-total list, against 33 with no target, because four Game Changers take most of the money.
  - Shared code still names no game rule: what a list is short of, and which cards count, are the adapter's answers (`ShellGoals.missing`, as the build-up asks).
- **Three cuts of the rule, and what each measured.** The first cut asked the bracket check about every protected card in every round, and took 89 to 97 ms at the median for a Bracket 4 list at $50 or $100 (233 ms at worst). A faster cut passed over protected cards until the count of Game Changers moved. It was not the same rule: 132 of 5,130 lists differed, and 18 lost a Bracket 4 they had kept. The cause was a list whose bracket is held by a second road (a combo, or land denial): while the Game Changers were passed over, the pieces of that road were swapped out as ordinary picks. The same read showed a flaw in the first cut: a card that held the target by another road could leave for a Game Changer that did not make the list whole. As built, the priciest protected card is judged again every round, every one of them before a run ends, and a stand-in for a needed card must make the list whole. Against the first cut it keeps every target that cut kept and 34 more, and it plans a Bracket 4 list at $100 in 25 ms at the median (60 ms at worst) on the owner's machine.
- **The route** (`POST /api/decks/autofill`). One optional field, `totalUsd`, with the bounds of a stored total.
  - It is its own field, never part of `goals`. The helper every snapshot body shares (`suggestionGoals`, `src/lib/decks/goals.ts`) still leaves the total out, so Suggestions and Swap Lab are not asked again by a new total, and a body with no total is byte for byte what it was.
  - With it the route prices what never leaves from the facts it already reads, and reads the basics' rows before the plan where it read them after: the same one statement, moved. No statement is added and there is no migration.
  - The answer gains `deck` (the total, the whole deck's figure, the cards with no known price, and the kept cards' share), and only with a total. The note and the sheet's footer both read that figure.
  - The notes about what a target kept out are still counted against a list planned with no total, so what the budget took out is never blamed on the target. The build-up's notes are counted after the fit.
  - With `ownedOnly` the total is ignored, as the per-card budget is (decision 5).
  - Caching intent unchanged: dynamic and never stored.
- **The words, and where each lives.**
  - *Over the total* is the shared planner's (`overTotalNote`), sent in the answer's `notes`. Either "With these added the deck comes to about $131, $31 over your $100 deck total. Autofill found no cheaper cards that fit." or, where the commander and the kept cards alone cost more than the total, "Your commander and the 12 cards you kept come to about $70, already over your $50 deck total. Autofill picked the cheapest cards that fit, and the deck comes to about $133." The noun is the adapter's. Cards with no known price are left out of the figure and counted in a closing sentence.
  - *A target left short* is Magic's (`mtgReachNotes`), through the third addition to the game contract that decision 9 covers: the note takes the total and names it, beside a per-card budget when both narrowed the list.
  - *The sheet's and the toast's* are in `src/lib/recommend/total-copy.ts`, with a test that reads every line.
- **The sheet** (`src/components/editor/autofill-sheet.tsx`).
  - The control is the Fit sheet's own field, moved into one component both sheets draw (`src/components/editor/deck-total-field.tsx`). Typing commits nothing; Set, Enter or a chip does. Anything that is not whole dollars from 1 is refused with the Fit sheet's line.
  - It is the last item of the controls row. The row the sheet had (Budget, the boxes, Reroll) stays as it was, and the control takes the line under it: at 476 px it cannot share Budget's line in a 640 px row. Placed straight after Budget, as first built, it sent Reroll to a third line by itself (the controls were 120 px tall on a desktop, against 84 px now and 48 px before the package). A test pins the order.
  - The total is the sheet's own until Add, like the target: opening, setting and Cancel write nothing.
  - While "Only cards I own" is ticked the control is switched off with its value still in view (decision 5): no total is sent and Add hands none over. It follows `ownedOnly`, as Budget does.
  - The read-only goals line no longer names the total. It shows for a per-card budget alone.
- **The editor.** Add hands the sheet's total over with the list, and the editor saves it when it differs from the deck's, in the same goals edit as a picked target: a new deck's one create carries it, a saved deck sends one PATCH beside its one PUT. Undo takes the cards back and leaves the total.
- **Tests.** `pnpm check` green: 4,250 tests in 336 files (4,179 in 334 at `e21abfd`, the commit under it), lint at the baseline's five warnings. The 71 new ones:
  - the planner (23): the fit on a small declaration where every figure is worked out by hand; the figure and the note, word for word;
  - a target under a total, over the real Magic adapter (13): the target's cards leave last; the total wins where both cannot hold; a cheaper card that counts stands in; one comes back from another group; the rest makes room or the try is undone; a card that holds the target by another road is not traded away; the ceiling holds through every swap; nothing is sampled;
  - the route (10), Magic's note (1), the sheet's words (7), the sheet (13) and the editor (4).
  - Untouched: the planner's fixed-seed golden, the route's "no-goals plan exactly" and the sheet's request-body pins. No assertion of an existing test was removed or changed but for one sheet pin, rewritten on purpose and in place: Y8's "a total reads its running estimate on the line, and never rides the body". It held both things the plan names: the total riding the body, and the goals line naming it.
  - One-line mutations, each run against its file's tests on the tree as committed: 65, of which 63 turn a test red. The two left change nothing a player or a caller can observe. One lets the read-only goals line draw for a total alone: the line is handed the goals without their total, so it draws nothing either way. The other has Add save a total that did not change: the goals come out equal, and the editor sends a goals write only when they differ from what it last saved. Four of the 63 survived a first run and each got a test: a stand-in that need not be cheaper, a card the target needs leaving for any cheaper card, a card with no price coming back in under the total, and a land the target needs giving its slot to a basic.
- **The plan's eight acceptance lines**, each by the check it names.
  1. The first example lands and the footer says so: the smoke's Etali check (the answer's figure for the whole deck is at or under 100, and it is the sum of the answer's own cards and the commander), and the sheet test "the footer reads the whole deck against the total".
  2. The second example's note: the planner's tests of the note's words and of the plan's edge case, and the smoke's Sheoldred check.
  3. The same total twice gives the same list: the planner test "nothing is sampled: Math.random never runs, and the same ask gives the same list".
  4. No total, no change: the existing pins, with no line of them touched.
  5. A deck with a total opens on it: the sheet test "a deck that already has a total opens on it: the ONE opening ask carries it, and the control reads it".
  6. After Add and a reload the goals line reads against the total and Fit my budget opens on it; after Undo the total remains: the editor's saved-deck test and its draft test.
  7. Cards with no known price are counted beside the figure: a planner test and a sheet test.
  8. A visitor sees no budget: the existing pin, and the walk-through's read below.
- **Verified against a local production build of this code**, on the dev branch.
  - `pnpm smoke:autofill`: 75 of 77. The two that fail are the dev branch's known ones ("topdeck evidence present for an aggregate-rich set" and "target 2: the shell's read stays inside it", LATER row 489), and the six new checks pass. Etali, Primal Storm at seed 424,242 with $100: 99 picks, no validation error, a whole deck of $97.21 summed from the answer's own cards, and the answer's figure is that number with no note about the total. The same seed with no total costs $1,040.57 and carries no figure; the fit brought in 29 cards, each marked, every other ranked pick is that list's, and every group holds what it held. Sheoldred, the Apocalypse with $100 cannot: the commander alone is $101.42 and the deck $118.96, and one note says so: "Your commander comes to about $101, already over your $100 deck total. Autofill picked the cheapest cards that fit, and the deck comes to about $119." `pnpm smoke:collection`, which asks Autofill four times: 93 of 93. It was started 24 seconds after the first ended, not the minute the limiter's note asks for; no ask was limited.
  - `pnpm build`: the route table is the one `0d59119` builds, line for line (both built clean and compared; `e21abfd` changes records only). The main stylesheet is 75 bytes larger, 171,824 to 171,899, and the second is the same file (5,721 bytes, the same name).
- **Read in the browser pane**, on the real sheet as a guest (a new deck with Krenko, Mob Boss; the sheet needs no sign-in for this), against the dev branch. The contrast figures and the switched-off look are from the dev server, read before the control moved to the end of its row (the move changes no color). Every size is from a local production build of the code as committed, with the tab in front.
  - One ask with `totalUsd: 100`, then "Deck total: est. $99.54 of your $100" and the line about what Add will save.
  - At 320, 390, 768 and 1280 px in both themes: nothing reaches past the sheet's edges and the page does not scroll sideways.

    | Width | The control | Chips | The controls: before this package, with no total, with $100 picked | The list on the first screen: the same three |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | 320 × 720, touch | two rows, 94 px | 44 px tall, 44 to 59 px wide | 134, 236, 272 px | 54, 0, 0 px |
    | 390 × 844, touch | two rows, 94 px | the same | 134, 236, 272 px | 218, 116, 80 px |
    | 768 × 1024 | one row, 28 px | 24 px tall | 48, 84, 104 px | 517, 481, 461 px |
    | 1280 × 800 | one row, 28 px | 24 px tall | 48, 84, 104 px | 327, 291, 271 px |

    "Before this package" was read with the same reader: the two phones on `23afe59`'s dev server and the wider two on a production build of `0d59119`, which did not touch the sheet. The third figure is taller by the line about what Add will save. The list's share was read once the footer's bracket line had loaded; on the dev branch that line is at its longest ("At least Bracket 4 (Optimized) · Couldn't check Game Changers, mass land denial, extra turns and combos"), so on a phone these are the tightest readings the footer gives. Production's line was read at the ship, on a 1,024 px window only (the Production bullet): one line there.
  - Contrast, dark and light: the label, the line under the controls and the footer's figure 6.86 to 1 and 6.67 to 1; a pressed chip 11.43 and 11.12; a resting chip 13.42 and 14.22.
  - The switched-off look was read by disabling the control's elements in the page: half opacity, the total and the pressed chip still in view. The wiring to "Only cards I own" is the sheet test's.
  - *Add, Undo and a reload, on a guest deck* (deleted afterwards: 204, then 404).
    - A new deck, $100, Add: one create that carries the total and one write of the cards, no goals write. The toast: "Added 99 cards", "Deck total set: $100. Undo takes the cards back, not the total." The deck's goals line then read "Your goals: est. $99.56 of your $100".
    - With the list put back to the commander through the cards route and the page loaded again, the sheet opened on the saved total: $100 pressed, 100 in the box, one ask with `totalUsd: 100`, and no line about saving. Add with the total unchanged sent the cards alone and its toast had no second line; Undo sent the one card back, and the deck's goals on the server were as before.
    - Not set, then Add: "Adding these cards removes this deck's total.", the footer back to the picks' own estimate, the cards and one goals write of none. The toast: "Deck total removed. Undo takes the cards back, not the total."
    - $100 again on the deck with no total, Add, and Undo 0.4 s later: the list's two edits cancelled before any save, so no cards were written at all, and the one write was the total's. The server then held one card and the $100 total.
    - Read with no token, as a visitor would: the same deck's goals came back empty, and its public page held no budget.
- **Decisions the build made** (the owner can flip any).
  1. *A chip clears the total.* "Not set" sits before the presets and reads pressed while there is none. The plan names no way back to no total; the Fit sheet has its own "Remove this deck's total".
  2. *A line says what Add will save, before the press*, as the target's does, and adds who sees it.
  3. *Clearing a saved total in the sheet and pressing Add removes it from the deck.* The toast says "Deck total removed."
  4. *The footer names its subject*: "Deck total: est. $97 of your $100", where the plan wrote the figure alone. The picks' own estimate ("Est. total $1543 (cheapest printings)") counts the picks; this one counts the whole deck.
  5. *No note when the list lands.* The footer carries the figure. How many picks the fit changed is not said (LATER row 533).
  6. *The line under "Only cards I own" is unchanged*: "Budget is off. Price limits don't apply to cards you own." It is the reason both controls name, and it names Budget alone (LATER row 535). Changing it would have meant rewriting a pin the plan does not name.
  7. *A needed card's stand-in keeps the build-up's mark*, so the note's count of what the build-up brought is the cards the list holds.
  8. *The build-up under the total takes the best-ranked card that fits*, not the cheapest, so a dear Game Changer can leave less for the rest of the list than a cheap one would (LATER row 534).
  9. *The control takes the line under the row, after Reroll in reading order.* The plan says "beside Budget", and nothing that wide fits beside it. On a phone the order is Budget, the boxes with Reroll, then Deck total over two lines.
- **Where the build differs from the plan, or falls short of it.**
  - *The fit keeps the target where it can.* The plan's fit is the plain rule, with the note worked out again. The owner chose the other at the gate, with the figures above.
  - *On a small phone the list starts below the first screen.* The control adds two rows of 44 px. At 320 × 720 on touch 54 px of the list showed before this package and none shows now, with or without a total; at 390 × 844 it was 218 px and is 116 px, or 80 px while the line about what Add will save shows. The sheet scrolls as one and the footer stays pinned, so nothing is unreachable (LATER rows 466 and 532). On a desktop the control costs one row of 36 px.
  - *A pick leaves only for a card of its own group.* Where a group holds no cheaper candidate the pick stays, however dear. In the corpus that is why the cheapest list is what it is; in a pool smaller than the deck (a test fixture) it can leave a list over a total a looser rule would reach (LATER row 531).
- **Not verified.**
  - On production: Add and everything after it (the read below pressed no Add), any phone width, and anything signed in.

- **A fix after the ship: every run of the fit starts from every card not in the list, and the build-up's floor counts a card that counts at the cheapest of its group that counts** (2026-11-26, the commit after `06e1b7a`). The owner's planning session reviewed the shipped fit against the plan and found a gap, and the owner asked for it to be fixed in this package's session. With a bracket target, a list could end over its total although a list under it existed, and the note then said that no cheaper card fit.
  - *The cause.* The cards a run of the fit took out stayed out of every later run, so the run where the total wins could not put a card that the run with the target kept had taken out in a Game Changer's place. The smallest case: two slots, a $50 card the target needs and a $5 card, with a $3 and a $1 card ranked behind them. At a $10 total the first run took the $5 card out for the $3 one, and that for the $1 one, and still cost $51. The second run had no stand-in left for the $50 card, so the list ended at $51 with the note "Autofill found no cheaper cards that fit", where $6 was in reach.
  - *The first change* (`fitDown` in `src/lib/recommend/autofill.ts`). A card a run takes out stays out for the rest of that run only. That keeps a run's candidates getting fewer, which is what lets it pass over a pick with nothing cheaper left. Every run starts from every card not in the list: the run where the total wins, and each run that makes room for the build-up under the total. A list with no target has one run, so it is untouched.
  - *A second gap, which the corpus showed once the first was closed.* The build-up's floor is the check that skips a try the fit cannot bring under the limit, and it held each card that counts at its own price. But the fit may swap a card the target needs for a cheaper card that counts, so the floor could be more than the fit can reach, and a try that would fit was skipped. With the first change alone, Ayara, First of Locthwain at $100 kept three Game Changers where the shipped fit had kept four. The build-up took Necropotence at $24.49, and the floor of the next try held it there, though Opposition Agent counts at $19.30 and would have made room. Bracket 4 showed on 4 fewer lists at $100 and 1 fewer at $150. As fixed, the floor prices each card that counts at the cheapest card of its group that counts, where that is less.
  - *Tests* (`src/lib/recommend/autofill-total.test.ts`, the last block). The reported case. The floor's case: four one-slot groups and a target of two, where after the total wins the build-up takes a $9 card that counts, and the next one fits only if that gives way to a $4 one. And 3,000 seeded cases with shallow pools: one to three mana values of one to three slots, up to two more candidates a slot, a fifth of the cards counting, a target of one or two, a total from 3 % to 130 % of the planned list. As shipped, 25 of those lists ended over a total that the cheapest list of their own shape reached, and 195 ended over their total while dearer than that cheapest list, each of them saying no cheaper card fit. As fixed, none does either. All three tests fail on the shipped planner and pass here.
  - *The corpus,* run again on the dev branch with both changes and compared list for list with the shipped run (`READ_AT=2026-10-27T14:00:00Z`, 5,130 lists). The 1,710 lists with no target are the same, byte for byte. Every landing count is the same: with a target of 3 or 4, as without one, 255, 280, 285 and 285 of 285 lists land at or under $50, $100, $150 and $250. With a target, 1,068 of 3,420 lists changed.
    - The 70 lists over their total cost no more than the cheapest list the rule reaches for the same commander, seed and target (its $1 total). 58 of them are cheaper than before, by up to $0.60, and 12 cost the same. Before, those 58 said that no cheaper card fit when one did.
    - At the $1 total, the cheapest list each rule reaches, 487 of 570 lists are cheaper, by up to $2.60. 10 are dearer by at most $0.02: the basics' own prices against the price the fit counts them at.
    - Bracket 3 shows as often as before: on 245, 280, 285 and 285 lists. Bracket 4 shows on 74, 229, 273 and 283, where it was 71, 226, 274 and 283. 8 lists gained it and 3 lost it, all of them under their total either way.
    - The picks that differ from the same seed's list with no total are as many as before, at the median at every total and target. Planning time is about the same: a Bracket 4 list at $50 plans in 30 ms at the median and 64 ms at worst, where it was 28 and 77.
  - *Checks.* `pnpm check` is green: 4,253 tests in 336 files (three new), lint at the baseline's five warnings. Of twenty one-line mutations of the fit and of the fix, nineteen turn a test red, among them the two that restore the shipped behaviour. The one left lets a card back into the same run that took it out, and changes nothing the tests or the corpus can see (see "What is left" below). A production build has `0d59119`'s route table and the same two stylesheets as `3658cb6`. `pnpm smoke:autofill` was not run again: none of its checks asks with a target and a total together, and the route is unchanged.
  - *The three lists that lost Bracket 4* were Krenko, Tin Street Kingpin at $100 on two seeds, and Sheoldred, Whispering One at $150. When the total wins, a Game Changer gives way to the best-ranked cheaper card of its group, as every pick does. With the first run's cards back in reach, that can be a dear one. On Krenko's list The One Ring gave way to Roaming Throne at $46.64, where the shipped fit, blocked, took Aetherflux Reservoir at $20.26. Each Game Changer that leaves then saves less, so three left where two had, and the build-up brought two back. Preferring the cheapest stand-in in that run would change the plan's rule, so it is the owner's call (LATER row 534).
  - *What is left.* Within one run, a card the run took out still does not come back. A run takes the dearest pick with a cheaper stand-in first, so that can only matter where a pick was passed over while a cheaper card of its group left: a card the target needs, or one whose cheaper cards all break the ceiling or the colorless cap on the base. No test or corpus list shows it.
  - *Production* (2026-11-26). The owner's word, in the build's session: "Merge and push", after being told the push carries `9d4b6cf` (package 3's walk-through record), `06e1b7a` (package 4's ship note) and `b836689` (this fix). ### A Maybe list under the deck (MAYBE-LIST, 2026-11-26, the first follow-up of `OWNER-NOTES-2026-11-24-session-prompt.md`, the owner's idea 3 of 2026-11-24: "Add a maybe section for cards you are considering also have the button in suggestion tabs still allow to add past 100 cards and it will add it to the maybe list at the bottom of the deck"; `1144a85`)

- **Why it exists.** Players had nowhere to park a card they were considering, and at 100 cards a Suggestions row reads "Swap in…", so a full deck could not take a suggestion without choosing a cut. Other builders' exports already carry a Maybeboard, which the importer read and dropped.
- **What a player sees.** Under the deck, after Tokens, a "Maybe" heading with its count, closed until opened (the open state is kept with the view preferences). Opened: "Cards you’re considering. They don’t count toward the deck and aren’t checked.", then one text row per card with its quantity, the owned mark and the cost pips, and no legality mark of any kind. An editor row carries "Move to deck" (an arrow; at the deck's maximum it is "Swap in…" and opens Y7b's sheet) and "Remove … from Maybe", revealed on hover and always shown at 44 px on touch, as the deck's own row actions are. Every Suggestions row has "Maybe" at the end of its sources line, at any deck size; a parked card's row reads "In Maybe" and keeps its Add ("Swap in…" at 100), which moves it into the deck. A search row marks a parked card "In Maybe". The phone card sheet gives a Maybe card "Move to deck" ("Swap in…" at the maximum) and "Remove from Maybe", and no tag editor. Visitors see the list on the share page under the same closed heading, read-only, with "Cards the builder is considering. They’re not part of the deck." A deck with Maybe cards and none in the deck reads "No cards yet / Add them from Search, or move one in from Maybe."
- **The owner's answers** (2026-11-26 05:28 Z, the question dialog, the default on each): Maybe beside "Swap in…" at any size (decision 1); a Maybe edit is no change to the list played (decision 2; the sketch gave no default); visitors see it closed and exports leave it out (decisions 3 and 4, put as one question); and, a choice step 0 turned up, "Replace deck" replaces the Maybe list only when the paste brings Maybe lines. Step 0 found the sketch's eleven places and nothing outside them that needs a change, so by the plan's rule it stayed one package; the brief is `MAYBE-LIST-session-prompt.md`.
- **The zone** (`src/lib/games/mtg/formats.ts`): `maybeboard`, labelled "Maybe", min 0, max null, `countsTowardSize: false`, no `isLeaderZone`, no `holds`, last after Tokens. Its id is the word the decklist parser already wrote for "Maybeboard" headers and Archidekt's out-of-deck categories. Shared code finds it by its shape, never its id: `maybeZoneOf` (`src/lib/decks/playable.ts`), the zone that counts toward no size, is no leader's and holds cards; a test reads every adapter's formats and pins at most one per format. One Piece declares none (LATER row 509), so every Maybe surface is absent there. No migration (a deck card's zone is free text checked against the declared zones), no new route, no new field in the game contract.
- **What never sees it, by construction.** `playable()` was already "leader OR counted", so `toDeckSnapshot` (validate, analyze, the bracket read, the speed facts, the export, Copy list, "At the table"), `deckSizeCount` and the progress line, `deckOwnership` and `deckEstimate`, the buy menu, precon changes, every request body built by `snapshotBody`, the Autofill sheet's kept list, `loadDeckEntries` through `playableEntries`, the combos route and the panels' fetch keys all read the deck alone. Each now has a test that a Maybe card changes nothing there: a banned, an off-color and a duplicate card in Maybe raise no issue and leave the size at 100, analytics, the export and the bracket read are those of the deck alone, and a Game Changer in Maybe moves nothing (it would in the deck).
- **The places step 0 found, and what changed in each.**
  1. *The link preview* (`src/lib/og/data.ts`) read every non-token row, so a Maybe card would have been counted, curved and priced. It now names the deck's playable zones (the format through the registry, `deckFormat`, moved into `src/lib/decks/deck-format.ts` so the preview and the version writers can use it without the route helpers' auth imports).
  2. *The editor's in-deck count* summed every zone; it fed the search row's ×N, Suggestions' "In deck ✓" and three Combo Radar reads, one of them the missing pieces "Add N pieces" adds. It now counts the deck alone (`playableQty`), and a second set (`maybeCardIds`) marks parked cards. A piece parked in Maybe is a missing piece again.
  3. *The list pane and the share page* drew only the token zone's rows out of the entries set aside; Maybe rows were kept and saved but never drawn. Both now draw the Maybe section (`src/components/deck/maybe-section.tsx`, the text view's rows through two new hooks on `DeckTextView`: `rowAction` and `removeLabel`).
  4. *The phone card sheet and the tag editor* took the first non-leader entry, which could have been a Maybe row. A Maybe entry is never the sheet's entry or the tag editor's now (a token keeps both, as before).
  5. *The list hash* covered every zone. It now leaves the Maybe zone out when it is given the deck's format (decision 2), and every caller that knows the deck gives it: `insertVersion` reads the format off the version bump's own `RETURNING`, the journal is handed the deck row by its route, and the versions route and the History dialog hold it. No stored hash moves, since no stored list held a Maybe row (the save route refused an undeclared zone). The diff still shows Maybe changes, and versions freeze the Maybe list.
  6. *Import's Replace* kept every row outside the playable list. It now replaces the Maybe list when the paste brings Maybe lines, and keeps it otherwise, as Tokens are kept.
  7. *The empty deck* read "Add them from Search." beside a Maybe list; its hint now names Maybe.
  8. *The guest-deck purge* calls a deck empty only when it has no row, so a Maybe-only guest deck is not empty. Kept on purpose and recorded (LATER row 547).
  9. *The save route's 500-row bound* counts every zone, and Maybe has no size of its own. `addCard` now refuses a new row past 500 lines with a sentence ("A deck can hold 500 lines at most, Maybe and Tokens included"), and Import skips the lines past it with one warning; before, the editor would have failed every save with nothing said.
  10. *The add paths* never took a card out of Maybe. See the next bullet.
  11. *Comments* in ten files that said Tokens was the only such zone are rewritten.
- **The editor's rule: a card that enters the deck leaves Maybe**, on every path, and each Undo puts the Maybe row back where it stood (pure helpers in `src/lib/decks/editor-state.ts`: `leaveMaybe` takes the rows with their starting indexes, `restoreRows` puts them back in index order, `enteredDeck` names the cards whose copies in the deck rose).
  - Search, the phone sheet's Add and a leader pick (`handleAdd`), and a Suggestions or Combo Radar row's Add (`handleUndoableAdd`): the Maybe row is taken before the add, so a deck at 500 lines can still move a card in, and the toast gains "Taken out of Maybe."
  - A combo's pieces together: the pieces that landed leave Maybe in one step after the adds, one toast, one Undo for all.
  - Swap in… and Swap Lab (`swapOne`): the incoming card leaves Maybe; the Undo restores the outgoing row and the Maybe row in their starting order, whichever stood first.
  - Whole-list changes (Import, Autofill's Add, Fit, Swap to fit, Upgrade toward, through `applyListSwap`): every card whose copies in the deck rose leaves Maybe; the Undo restores the previous list whole, as before. Autofill's Add keeps the Maybe rows as it keeps Tokens.
  - "Move to deck": below the maximum the row's copies go to the main list with the printing the row chose, one toast ("Moved … to the deck"), one Undo for both; at the maximum it opens Swap in…. A Maybe add refuses a card the deck plays or one already parked, with its sentence.
- **Import** (`src/lib/decks/import.ts`). A pasted Maybeboard section and Archidekt's `{noDeck}` categories land in Maybe with no parser change; Sideboard and Companion lines stay skipped ("No "sideboard" zone in Commander: skipped …"). A Maybe line for a card the deck holds after the import is left out with one warning ("Already in the deck, so left out of Maybe: …"), and a Maybe row whose card the paste puts in the deck leaves quietly.
- **Tests.** `pnpm check` green: 4,313 tests in 336 files (4,250 in 336 at `1b1cddc`), 63 new, lint at the baseline's five warnings. New cases in the playable list, the pure edits, Import, the decklist, the hash, the versions writer, the versions and games routes, the link preview's statement, the view preferences, the History dialog, the Suggestions panel, the list pane, the share page, the card pane and the editor (13: Maybe at 100 / 100 with one PUT and no new Suggestions request, a reload with a banned off-color card in Maybe, Move to deck with its printing and its Undo, Swap in… from Maybe, a search add of a parked card, a Suggestions add of one, Remove from Maybe, the phone sheet, the Card tab's tag editor, Import's Maybeboard and the export, an import and an Autofill Add that take a parked card in, and a combo's parked piece). No assertion of an existing test was removed; two were changed on purpose: the list pane's heading order gains "Maybe0" after "Tokens0", and the link preview's card statement binds the zone list after the token document (its row fixture gains the format id).
  - One-line mutations of every zone check, each run against its file's tests: 38, all caught (two survived the first run, a tag-editor pick and the section's swap icon, and each got a test). The harness and its output are in the side-session notes (`MAYBE-LIST-evidence/`).
- **Verified against a local production build of this code**, on the dev branch.
  - `pnpm smoke:decks` 65 of 65, 7 new: a Maybe row saves and reads back in its zone; an off-color and a banned card there are named by none of the server's validation and leave the leader denorm alone; a token there is refused.
  - `pnpm smoke:versions` 61 of 61, 13 new: a version counts 5 cards with a Maybe row beside them, freezes the row, and is stamped with the hash of the deck alone; a game logged then reuses it, and so does one logged after a Maybe-only edit; History's hash matches the live list; a fork copies the Maybe list; a card moved from Maybe into the deck makes the next game mint a played list; a restore brings the Maybe row back.
  - `pnpm smoke:games` 63 of 63 (the journal's entry point changed). Its first try met the local deck-create limiter after the two smokes above; `pnpm counters:reset` cleared it on the dev branch and the second try passed.
  - `pnpm build`: the route table is `1b1cddc`'s, line for line (both built clean; 85 lines). Both stylesheets are the same files (171,899 and 5,721 bytes, the same names); client scripts are 9,641 bytes larger in all. The preview route's traced server code is 472,533 bytes, from 403,647 (it now loads the adapters; LATER row 546).
- **Read in the browser pane** (hidden, driven by script, as the project's notes say), on a guest deck that Autofill filled to 100: at 1280 px a Suggestions row's Maybe gave one resolve and one PUT, no new Suggestions request, "100 / 100 cards", "Maybe1" and "In Maybe"; after a reload the deck read 100 / 100, the Warden still approved it, and the section opened on its note and a row with "Swap in Goblin Chieftain…" and Remove, no dot. At 1280 and 768 px (fine pointer) and at 390 and 320 px (touch), in both themes, nothing scrolled sideways; on touch the disclosure, the swap and the remove are 44 px and always shown, the row 48 px, the name unclipped at 320 px; the phone sheet offered "Swap in…" and "Remove from Maybe", 44 px each, and no tag editor; Suggestions rows at 320 px keep "Swap in…" on the name's line and "Maybe" (56 × 44 px) on the sources line. The share page read "100 / 100 cards" with "Maybe1" closed, and the link preview image (served locally at its `og:image` path) "100 cards" and "$1,617", the page's own estimate without the Maybe card. On touch the row first got 14 px shorter when "Maybe" turned into "In Maybe"; the mark now keeps the button's 44 px, and the row read 132 px before and after the press.
- **Read in WebKit** (the iOS Simulator's Safari, an iPhone 15, against the same build): the share page with "Maybe 1" closed, opened by a tap; a fresh guest deck filled by Autofill to 100, a Suggestions row's "Maybe" pressed ("Added Goblin Chieftain to Maybe · Undo", "In Maybe", the tab still "Deck · 100"), and the Deck tab's Maybe section opened on its note and the row with its two controls in the deck rows' column. The three test decks were deleted afterwards; the dev branch holds none of them.

### Maybe from the search rows too (MAYBE-LIST amended, 2026-11-26, the owner's ask after the ship, on a phone screenshot of the editor's search at 100 / 100: "as well as the suggestion tab id like to add the maybe button to add cards from the search bar on the deck builder as well"; `14d415c`)

- **What a player sees.** A search result whose card is in neither the deck nor Maybe has a "Maybe" button between the main add and the leader's: "Add · Maybe · Commander" below the deck's maximum, "Swap in… · Maybe · Commander" at it, where it parks the card without a swap (the owner's decision 1, carried over from Suggestions). It parks the box's quantity ("2 Goblin Bomb" parks two, and the toast reads "Added 2× Goblin Bomb to Maybe" with its Undo), and like an add it clears the box and takes focus back, so the next search starts at once; a refusal (the 500-line bound) lands on the pane's live line and the query stays. A parked card's row keeps its "In Maybe" mark and offers no Maybe; a card the deck plays shows its ×N and none. The button is the row's own kind (`secondary`, 24 px, 44 px on touch), and a press on it is not a tap on the row: it inspects nothing and opens no sheet.
- **Supersedes, in the section above.** "What a player sees": search rows carry the button now. "Left for later": row 538 is narrowed to the Combo Radar rows and the phone card sheet. The open, empty section's line reads "Nothing here yet. Search and Suggestions can park a card here with Maybe; it won’t count toward the deck."
- **The code.** `SearchPane` (`src/components/editor/search-pane.tsx`) takes `onMaybe(card, qty)` and shows the button on a row only while the deck holds no copy (`owned === 0`) and the card is not in `inMaybe`; the add's and the Maybe's outcomes share `settleAdd` (a refusal on the live line, otherwise the box cleared and focused). The editor passes `handleMaybeAdd` only for a format with a Maybe zone, as it does to Suggestions, so One Piece has no button; `handleMaybeAdd` takes a quantity (Suggestions still parks one), and `MAYBE_COPY.added` names a quantity above one. No key parks a card: Enter and Ctrl/Cmd+Enter keep their adds, and a keyboard reaches the button with Tab from the box (the selected row's "Swap in…" or "Add", then "Maybe"), whose Enter parks the card and returns focus to the box (LATER row 549).
- **Tests.** `pnpm check` green: 4,321 tests in 336 files (4,313 at `635c746`), 8 new, lint at the baseline's five warnings. ### Maybe as pictures, with its way in in words (MAYBE-LIST amended again, 2026-11-26, the owner's ask on a screenshot of the editor's Maybe list at 100 / 100: "i want to add the feature to view the maybe list in the grid format as well so you can view the cards better and then have a swap button as well down there next to the cards to click when you decide you want to swap those cards into the deck from your maybe list"; `88c506c`)

- **What a player sees.** The Maybe section follows the deck pane's View, as Tokens do: in the grid each Maybe card is a tile, the printed card at the deck's tile size, with its ×N badge (named "1 in Maybe"), and under the image a button in words, "Swap in…" at the deck's maximum (it opens the swap sheet, which cuts one card for it) or "Move to deck" below it (the card's copies come in), with an ✕ beside it that takes the card out of Maybe (its toast has Undo). The tile's own click shows the card, as every tile's does. In the list, each Maybe row now shows the same button in words, always, where it was an icon that appeared only when the pointer hovered the row; the row's ✕ keeps the deck rows' reveal. The share page's Maybe section follows its View too: read-only tiles, no buttons.
- **The owner's answer** (2026-11-26, one question, before the build): the list's button is "Labeled button, always (Recommended)", over an icon always shown or the list left as it was; the option said the Maybe rows' cost column would sit left of the deck rows'. Not asked, the defaults: the tile gets a remove beside its button (a tile's click only shows the card, and the computer's Card tab has no actions, so without it a grid could not take a card out of Maybe), and the share page follows the View.
- **On touch the list's Maybe row takes two lines** (the build's own call, not the owner's): inline, the worded button (102 px with "Move to deck") and its 44 px ✕ would have left a 320 px phone's names about 54 px; on a second line, right-aligned, the names keep their width (208 px at 320, 278 at 390, none cut). The first line keeps a 44 px target: the name holds it (`pointer-coarse:min-h-11`), so a Maybe row is 96 px on touch where a deck row is 48. A first build without that rule measured the name 20 px tall at 390 px; the read caught it.
- **Supersedes, in the MAYBE-LIST section.** "The cards are the text view's rows in either View" and "Move to deck … revealed on hover": tiles in the grid, and the button in words. Its "Where the build differs" note on the arrow icon, and LATER row 539 (fired, done). The phone card sheet is unchanged (Move to deck / Swap in… and Remove from Maybe).
- **The code.** `MaybeSection` (`src/components/deck/maybe-section.tsx`) takes the pane's `view`, renders `DeckGridView` in the grid with two new props, `tileFooter` (controls under each tile's image, in the tile's flow, never over the card, whose frame's bottom carries the artist / © line) and `countLabel` (the ×N badge's name), and in the list passes its worded button as the text view's `rowAction`. `DeckTextView` puts a row's action and its remove in one span (`data-slot="row-actions"`) and, on touch, wraps the row so that span takes a second line. `MAYBE_COPY` gains `swapIn` ("Swap in…") and `countLabel`. The pane and the share page pass `view`.
- **Tests.** `pnpm check` green: 4,330 tests in 336 files (4,321 at `4deaf70`), 9 new and two pins rewritten (the list's move was an icon on hover; it is the word, always shown), lint at the baseline's five warnings. ### Changes from your first full list (FIRST-LIST, 2026-11-26, `0d2d983`, the owner's ask with two screenshots of PRECON-CHANGES' sheet and line: "i want to add this same feature to view the list of changes from a pre con to any deck you built from the first time you get your 100 cards built if it can save that list and then when you go to upgrade it to a different bracket it will compare that list of the changes it makes"; LATER row 451)

- **The owner's four calls** (asked in the chat before any code, 2026-11-26; each the recommended answer). 1: the baseline is the **first full list**, saved the first time the list reaches its format's full size; Upgrade toward does not move it, so an upgrade's swaps show up as changes beside the ones made by hand (passed over: a baseline taken as Upgrade toward applies, which drops hand edits from the pull list and gives a deck never upgraded no line). 2: the sheet gets **"I've made these changes"**, which makes the list on screen the new baseline, for after the swaps are made in the physical deck. 3: the list lives in **its own spot**, not in History, never counted toward the 50 versions. 4: a deck **already full** when this shipped keeps the list as it stood **before its next edit**.
- **What a player sees.** On a deck with no precon lineage, under the leader zone where the precon's line would be: "No changes from your first full list yet." once the list is full (the moment the 100th card lands, before any save), then "Changed from your first full list: 2 out, 2 in." with "See changes". The rows the baseline did not hold carry the circled plus, named "Not in your first full list" (or "1 not in …"). "See changes" opens PRECON-CHANGES' sheet titled "Changes from your first full list", its lead "What to take out of your first full list and what to add to build this one.", the same cost line, Copy as text, Take out and Add, and one more section at its end: "Made these changes to your deck?", "Changes will count from the list as it is now.", and the button "I've made these changes". The button closes the sheet, the line reads "No changes since your list of Nov 26." with focus on its words, and the toast "Changes now count from this list" has an Undo that puts the previous baseline back, its day included. After that the line, title, lead and marks speak of "your list of Nov 26" ("Nov 26, 2025" outside the current year, in the reader's own time zone).
- **Storage.** Migration `0022` adds `deck_baselines` (deck_id primary key, ON DELETE CASCADE from decks; `source` 'complete' or 'owner'; `cards` jsonb `[{cardId, qty}]` folded per identity over the counted zones, the precon list's own shape; `set_at`). A table, not a `decks` column: `/account` and `/api/decks/mine` select whole deck rows, and a ~5 KB list would ride every listing (the owner's call 3 was "its own spot"; the chat said a column, and the table is that spot without the cost). Never copied on fork, not versioned. A 100-identity list is ~5 KB of JSON, one row per finished deck.
- **Who writes it.** The editor, the goals' way: `PATCH /api/decks/[id]` takes `baseline` (`baselineSchema`: source, an ISO `setAt`, at most 500 rows of a uuid and 1 to 999 copies; null deletes), folds and sorts the rows, stores a future `setAt` as now, upserts, and never moves `updated_at`; a baseline-only body writes no `decks` column. A deck with a precon lineage gets a 400 ("This deck counts its changes from its precon"), before anything is written. The owner's `GET` carries `deck.baseline` (null when none) and `baselineCards`, the CardWires of its identities the list no longer holds, so "Take out" is named on open with no request of its own; a visitor's wire has neither key and the server reads neither.
- **When it is set** (`firstListStamp` in `deck-editor.tsx`, one rule for `save()` and the keepalive copy). The save that first sends a full list stores it, its PATCH after the list's PUT (the owner's call 1). A deck that loads full with none takes the list it loaded, unsaved, and holds it (`baselineWaitsRef`) until a save sends the list itself, so a target or a rename alone writes no baseline and a load or a hidden tab with nothing to send writes nothing (the owner's call 4). A deck below full shows no line until it is full; after its baseline exists the line stays below full too.
- **Shared code.** `src/lib/decks/first-list.ts` (pure: `listComplete` over the counted zones and the format's `deckSize`, `baselineOf`, the schema and its normalizer, `baselinePatchBody`, `baselineDate`), `first-list-copy.ts` (the words in two voices), `baselines.ts` (the table's read and write). `precon-changes-sheet.tsx` exports `ChangesSheet` (title, lead, `namesFrom`: the precon's slug for its one GET, absent for a first list; `onReset`), and `PreconChangesSheet` is now its wrapper with the precon's words; the line takes `words` and `slot`; `DeckTextView` / `DeckGridView` take `beyondLabel`; dialog slot `"first-list-changes"`. The difference, the cost and Copy as text are `preconChanges`, `preconAddCost` and `preconChangesText` unchanged: the first list has the precon list's shape.
- **Tests.** `pnpm check` green: 4,348 tests in 338 files, lint at the baseline's six warnings (five before plus one in `scripts/.tmp`, not this change's). New: 9 for `first-list.ts`, 10 for the route (the upsert and its fold, the future clamp, null's delete, the precon 400, six malformed bodies, owner only; the owner's GET with its cut cards, none stored, a precon deck, a visitor), 8 in `deck-editor-first-list.test.tsx` (loaded full writes nothing, the first list edit stores the loaded list, the keepalive pair, reaching 100 in the editor, a stored baseline's line, marks and sheet with no request, the reset's one PATCH, focus, toast and Undo, an owner's day, a precon deck sends none). Changed on purpose: Upgrade toward's and Swap to fit's apply tests now expect the one baseline PATCH beside the PUT and the line "Changed from your first full list: 2 out, 2 in." after the swap (the owner's scenario: a full deck upgraded), and its Undo back to "No changes …" with no second PATCH; one Autofill test expects the loaded list's baseline beside its goals PATCH, two others read the goals PATCHes alone.
- **Read in the browser pane** (hidden, driven by script; `pnpm dev` on the dev branch after `pnpm db:migrate` applied `0022` there; one guest deck made for it). A blank draft importing Keen Engineering's 100 cards: the line "No changes from your first full list yet." in the render the import landed in; then POST 201, PUT 200 and the baseline PATCH 200 (`source` complete). After a reload the owner's GET held 67 rows, 100 copies; a visitor's had no `baseline` or `baselineCards` key. Two cards out and two in: "Changed from your first full list: 2 out, 2 in.", both new rows marked, later saves only PUTs. After a reload the sheet named both cut cards with no request on open ("The cards to add cost $1.06."). The reset: one PATCH (`source` owner), the line "No changes since your list of Nov 26.", focus on its words, no marks, the toast with Undo; an Undo restored the earlier baseline with its own `set_at`, read back from the GET. At 390 px the sheet is the Drawer, the reset button wraps under its words at 44 px; at 320 px the line's words wrap in their box beside a 72 × 44 px "See changes", the line 44 px; no sideways scroll; no server error.
- **`pnpm build`** in a clean worktree of `35511d0` with this change's files copied in: green, no route added (one route changed, `/api/decks/[id]`, still `ƒ`).
- **Not verified.** WebKit, Firefox, a real phone, One Piece (its 50-card size is read from the adapter; no OP deck was read), light theme, a signed-in owner, and production when this was written: the Production bullet below has what followed. Production needed migration `0022` applied **before** the code deployed: the owner's GET reads `deck_baselines` for every deck with no precon lineage, so the editor would fail to load such decks against a database without the table.
- **Production** (2026-11-26, on the owner's "Migrate, then push"). Migration `0022` went first, through the Neon MCP as ONE transaction on the `production` branch (`br-spring-king-avj6ye5p`, database `neondb`), after a read showed the migrations table ending at `0021`'s row (id 22, the dev branch's hash) and no `deck_baselines`: the file's two statements and the migrator's row (hash `7d8c9057…3233a`, `created_at` 1795709120953, the dev branch's row 23 byte for byte). Read back: the four columns as declared, the primary key, the cascade to `decks`, the source check, 0 rows, the last migrations row id 23. Then `git push origin a8b65ec:main` at 16:49:50 Z (`4deaf70..a8b65ec`: `35511d0`, `0d2d983`, `a8b65ec`; DECK-FACTS' `6bbc729` and `f9d799b`, committed on local `main` above it by another session meanwhile, stayed local); Vercel success 16:50:32 Z, CI run 45862347230 green 16:55:53 Z; Sentry held no error event from 16:50 Z to the read's end. **Read as a guest on the owner's OK** (17:04 to 17:06 Z; the browser pane, signed out, the header reading "Sign in", no deck key stored): a draft importing Keen Engineering's 100 cards (67 of 67 lines) showed "No changes from your first full list yet." in the render the import landed in; POST 201, PUT 200 and the baseline PATCH 200 (`source` complete) in the same second; the owner's GET held 67 rows and 100 copies, and a visitor's had neither new key. Adaptive Omnitool and Aether Spellbomb out, Fellwar Stone and Wayfarer's Bauble in: "Changed from your first full list: 2 out, 2 in.", both new rows "Not in your first full list", two PUTs. After a reload the sheet ("Changes from your first full list") named both cut cards from the GET's `baselineCards` with no API request on open, "The cards to add cost $1.02." "I've made these changes": the line "No changes since your list of Nov 26.", focus on its words, no marks, the toast "Changes now count from this list" with Undo, PATCH 200 (`source` owner); its Undo 1.3 s later put the line back at "2 out, 2 in.", PATCH 200, and the GET read the first list again with its own `set_at` (17:04:49.142 Z). Deleted through More, Details, "Delete deck…" (the native confirm answered by a one-call stub): DELETE 204, a hop to `/`, the key gone, the deck's GET 404, and production held 0 rows for it in `decks` and in `deck_baselines` (the cascade). Writes: one POST, three PUTs, three PATCHes, one DELETE, and the limiter's counters; page views: the draft, one reload, and the home page after the delete. ### Two counted facts about the list in the deck's statistics (DECK-FACTS, 2026-11-26, the second and last follow-up of `OWNER-NOTES-2026-11-24-session-prompt.md`, the owner's idea 2 of 2026-11-24: "if you could find out a way to have a metric of how fun the deck is, cause i’d rather have a fun deck than a boring one even if it’s better"; `6bbc729`)

- **Why it exists.** Nothing on the site said whether a list is its own thing or the same staples as every other deck. The owner's answer of 2026-11-24 settled what "fun" may mean here: facts about the list, not a score, not a rating per game, not a theme-first setting (LATER rows 499, 500 and 481 keep those). The rule it keeps is the bracket read's "never one authoritative number" (`src/lib/games/types.ts`): two counts, each with what it is out of and where it comes from, and no word that judges.
- **What a player sees.** Two more tiles at the end of the stat row in Analytics (after Avg. mana value, Lands and Est. price), in the editor's Analytics (the closed collapsible under the deck, and the phone's Analytics tab) and on the share page:
  - "Vampire cards / 34 of 63 / Nonland cards, by type line": the type the theme reader Suggestions uses finds first, and how many of the list's nonland cards carry it. A list the reader finds no type in (Atraxa's precon, Meren's) has no such tile: nothing is shown rather than a guess.
  - "Commander staples / 16 of 63 / Nonland cards in EDHREC's top 2,000": the nonland cards at EDHREC rank 2,000 or better, the cut-off Suggestions and the Cut Coach already call "A Commander staple in EDHREC decklists" (`STAPLE_RANK`). A card with no rank is not counted.
  Both are out of the same number, the nonland cards the curve and "Avg. mana value" count (the commander included), so "Lands" and "of N" add up to the deck. The share page's Why sheet still shows the player's own answer to "Staples and high card quality?"; the count and the answer may differ, and both stay.
- **Step 0, the measurement** (the dev branch, 2026-11-26, before any code; the brief is `DECK-FACTS-session-prompt.md`). The 182 precon rows (174 distinct lists, 56 to 71 nonland cards each): the share of each list's nonland cards at rank 100, 300, 500, 1,000, 2,000 and 5,000 or better. At 2,000 the median list has 38 %, the 10th to 90th percentile 24 to 53 % (15 to 34 cards), all of them 11.5 to 70.8 %, spread over seven ten-point bins with no pile at either end, and 1.82 times the spread chance alone would give (a binomial at the mean share over 62 cards). Ranks 100 to 500 put every precon under 40 % and barely beat chance (0.87 to 1.23); 5,000 spreads the precons further (2.52) but crowds staple-heavy lists at the top (a tenth of the dev branch's 50 full user decks at 97 % or more). By the sketch's rule, 2,000 stayed. The reader finds a type on 93 of the 174 lists (97 of the 182 rows; row 480's 85 rows have none), two or more on 21 of them. The scripts and their output are in the side-session notes (`DECK-FACTS-evidence/`).
- **The owner's answers** (2026-11-26 16:03 Z, the question dialog, the default on both): keep rank 2,000; the strongest type only (Kaalia of the Vast's precon reads "Angel cards: 11 of 62", and its Dragons and Demons stay in Suggestions' own rows; LATER row 558).
- **The code.** `analyzeMtg` (`src/lib/games/mtg/analyze.ts`) ends its blocks with two `stat` blocks, `deck-type` and `staples`; their words are `DECK_FACTS_WORDS`, in the adapter. The type is the first theme of `mtgThemes.read`, handed the list as the server's `snapshotThemes` hands it (copies summed per card, each commander also as a leader with its rules text), so the tile's number is the one a Suggestions row of that type prints (the row's "Shares the … type with N of your cards", or for a type the commander names, "N of your cards share it"). The analysis takes `toDeckSnapshot`'s playable list, so a token or a Maybe card is in neither count. No route, no migration, no field in the game contract (`popularity` is on every card the editor and the share page load), and the renderer (`AnalyticsBlocks`) is unchanged. One Piece's analysis is its own and unchanged.
- **Tests.** `pnpm check` green: 4,365 tests in 338 files (4,348 at `a8b65ec`, FIRST-LIST's), 17 new, lint at the baseline's five warnings (before the rebase onto FIRST-LIST: 4,338 in 336, from 4,321). Eleven are Magic's analysis: the two tiles and their place after Est. price; the cut-off pinned at 2,000 with its line; copies counted, rank 2,000 in and 2,001 or no rank out; a land in neither (a land at rank 2 is no staple, a land's own types no deck type); no type found, no tile, while the staples tile still counts; no nonland card, neither tile; several types, the strongest only; a land-fronted card in the command zone naming its back face's type, no tile rather than "0 of N"; a card of the 99 naming its own type, no deck type; a Maybe card and a token through `toDeckSnapshot`, no change; no judging word ("fun", "boring", "generic", "score"). Three run the server's own read, `snapshotThemes`, with only its one statement stubbed, against the tile, each with the row's own sentence: a type the commander names (copies and a back face counted), a type counted on the list, a card in two zones. One each covers the share page's stat row, the phone's Analytics tab, and One Piece's frozen block set with a rank on every card. One assertion of an existing test changed on purpose: the share page's "no h3 starting with Commander" (the old Leader block) leaves "Commander staples" out and now also finds that tile. Sixteen one-line mutations of the new lines (the rank test dropped, its boundary, unranked cards, copies, the staples tile on a list with no nonland card, both denominators, the last theme for the first, the leader's rules, every card read as a leader, copies summed per card and one copy per card, the count-0 guard, the two lines of words, the order): all caught, the guard once its test was added.
- **The corpus check.** The shipped `analyzeMtg`, run over the 182 precons on the dev branch through `toDeckSnapshot`, gives step 0's figures on all 182 (97 with a type tile; Vampiric Bloodline "Vampire cards: 34 of 63" and "Commander staples: 16 of 63"; Breed Lethality no type tile and "Commander staples: 25 of 61"; Heavenly Inferno "Angel cards: 11 of 62").
- **`pnpm build`** from clean, twice: with this change on `35511d0` (as `605ac8b`) and, after the rebase, on `a8b65ec` (FIRST-LIST's record, as `6bbc729`). Both route tables are `14d415c`'s line for line (85 lines; no commit between adds a route), so each is its starting commit's; both stylesheets are the same files each time (171,899 and 5,721 bytes, the same names); client scripts were 401 bytes larger than `14d415c`'s before the rebase. No route, query or write path changed, so no smoke was run.
- **Read in the browser pane** (hidden, driven by script, as the project's notes say; a local production build of `6bbc729` on the dev branch). The share page of Vampiric Bloodline (`/d/p_vampiric_bloodline_voc`) reads "Vampire cards / 34 of 63 / Nonland cards, by type line" and "Commander staples / 16 of 63 / Nonland cards in EDHREC's top 2,000" after Avg. mana value, Lands (37) and Est. price, and the same server's Suggestions for that precon gave 43 of its 50 rows "Has the Vampire type, which Strefan, Maurer Progenitor's rules text names" with "34 of your cards share it", the tile's number. Breed Lethality's page has no type tile and "Commander staples / 25 of 61" (39 lands). In the editor, on the precon's zero-write draft (`/decks/new?game=mtg&from=vampiric_bloodline_voc`), the Analytics under the list (1280 and 768 px) and the phone's Analytics tab (390 and 320 px) read the same tiles, and before the draft's list arrived the empty list showed neither. At every width in both themes nothing clipped and nothing scrolled sideways. On the share page the five tiles share one line at 1280 and 768 px, the two facts take a second line at 390 px and a line each at 320 px; in the editor's 640 px middle pane (1280) "Commander staples" alone wraps, in the 416 px pane at 768 px and at 390 px the two facts share the second line, and at 320 px each has its own. The tiles take the stat row's existing colors (light: titles rgb(86, 94, 91) and values rgb(36, 44, 47) on rgb(247, 244, 238); dark: rgb(169, 176, 173) and rgb(243, 241, 234)). The reads created no deck: a guarded count of the dev branch's decks created meanwhile found two untitled guest decks started from no precon, which a save of the draft would not make. After the rebase, a build of `6bbc729` read the same in the editor at 1280 px and on the share page at 390 px. Not read in WebKit or Firefox: the tiles are text in the row that already holds three.

### Maybe from Combos and the card sheet, a key for it, and two older fixes in search (MAYBE-LIST's open rows, 2026-11-26, the owner's ask: "Still open on the Maybe list (LATER.md): lets do what we can to fix them", naming LATER rows 538, 549, 550 and 551; `2aae9a8`)

- **What a player sees.**
  - **Combos (row 538).** A "One card away" row whose missing piece is in neither the deck nor Maybe has "Maybe" after its Add ("Swap in…" at 100 / 100, where Maybe parks the piece without a swap), a button of the Add's own kind (24 px, 44 px on touch). It parks one copy with the editor's toast ("Added Reiterate to Maybe" and its Undo), and every row whose missing piece is that card then reads "In Maybe" in its place; the row's Add still moves it into the deck. A piece the deck holds reads "In deck ✓" with neither. The commander rows' "Add N pieces" has no Maybe: it is a batch, and Maybe parks one card (LATER row 559).
  - **The phone card sheet (row 538).** A card in neither list, inspected from a search row or anywhere else that opens the sheet, has "Maybe" after "Add" ("Swap in…" at the maximum), an outline button like Remove and Swap…. It parks one copy, and the sheet stays on the card, turned to the Maybe card's own actions ("Move to deck", or "Swap in…" at the maximum, and "Remove from Maybe").
  - **A key (row 549).** In the search box Shift+Enter parks the selected card with the box's quantity, as the row's Maybe does: the box clears and keeps its focus. For a card the button would not offer, the editor's sentence lands on the live line ("Already in the deck", "Already in Maybe"). Without a Maybe list (One Piece) Shift+Enter is Enter, as before. The hint line names it, "⇧+Enter Maybe" ("Shift+Enter Maybe" off a Mac), and the `?` sheet has the row "Shift + Enter · Add the selected card to Maybe".
  - **Hover (row 550).** On a fine pointer a hovered result row no longer grows: its buttons take the place of its ×N and pips, on the name's line, and the name truncates under the pointer, so the row keeps its 44 px and nothing below it moves. Hover shows the main add and Maybe, not the leader's button; the selected row (a click, or the arrow keys) and every row on touch keep all three.
  - **The toast and an iPhone's keyboard (row 551).** While an on-screen keyboard is up, the editor's toasts stand above it. After an Add or a Maybe from search on an iPhone the box takes focus back and Safari raises the keyboard; "Added Sol Ring" and its Undo now sit above the keyboard and Safari's address pill instead of under them. With the keyboard down a toast is where it was, above the tab bar.
- **The owner's answers** (2026-11-26, the question dialog, both on row 550). Before the build: "Buttons at row end (Recommended)" over "No buttons on hover", on a preview that showed a hovered row as "Goblin Gu…" beside all three buttons. That preview overstated the name's room: measured with a real pointer in the 320 px pane the name kept 46 px below the maximum ("Gobli…") and 17 px at it ("A…"). Asked again with a screenshot of the "A…" row, the owner answered "Add + Maybe on hover (Recommended)" over keeping all three buttons and over no buttons on hover.
- **The build's own calls** (not the owner's): the combo row's Maybe as the Add's kind (secondary) and the sheet's as Remove's (outline); Shift+Enter handing a refusal to the live line rather than doing nothing; "Combos" named in the empty Maybe section's sentence, which now reads "Nothing here yet. Search, Suggestions and Combos can park a card here with Maybe; it won’t count toward the deck."; and the hint line's order and word. With "⇧+Enter add to Maybe" after "Enter" the line took a fourth row at 1280 px (61 → 81 px, the results 20 px lower), and off a Mac ("Shift", "Ctrl") it took one in every order with those words. As built it reads "↑ ↓ select · Enter add · ? shortcuts", then "⇧+Enter Maybe · “4 Name” sets quantity", then "⌘+Enter add as Commander": 61 px, the line's height before the change, in all four cases measured (a Mac's keys and the others', below the maximum and at it). "? shortcuts" moved up from the end; the leader's item, the longest, ends the line.
- **Supersedes.** FRESH-UI-EDITOR's bullet that the actions, "when hover shows them inline, take an implicit fourth column" (a grid makes no column for an item it does not place: they wrapped to a new row, LATER row 550). "Maybe from the search rows too": "No key parks a card" (row 549), and "Left for later" for rows 538, 550 and 551.
- **The code.**
  - `ComboRadarPanel` (`src/components/editor/combo-radar-panel.tsx`) takes `inMaybe` and `onMaybe`; a one-away row's Maybe goes through `useResolvedAdd`'s `maybe`, the same resolve and cache as the Add with its own pending card. The editor passes `handleMaybeAdd` only for a format with a Maybe zone, as it does to Suggestions and search.
  - `SheetActions.onMaybe` (`card-detail-pane.tsx`): the editor sets it for a card with no entry in any zone, in a format with a Maybe zone; it calls `handleMaybeAdd` for one copy and toasts a refusal.
  - `SearchPane`: Shift+Enter calls `maybe(card, qty)` when `onMaybe` is passed (Ctrl/Cmd wins over Shift); the hint line's item and order. `ShortcutsSheet` takes `maybe`. `MAYBE_COPY.keyHint` and `keyRow` are the words.
  - The row: the selected row's actions are placed on the second line at every pointer; an unselected row's are `hidden`, on touch the same second line, and on a fine pointer `group-hover` puts them in the first line's third column while the meta cell is `hidden`; the leader's button is `pointer-fine:hidden` on an unselected row. Before, hover showed the actions with no placement, and the grid's auto-placement wrapped them onto a new row in the 26 px thumb column.
  - `useKeyboardInset` (`src/components/editor/use-keyboard-inset.ts`, called by the editor) writes `--keyboard-inset` on the root from `visualViewport`: `innerHeight − offsetTop − height`, 0 while zoomed in, never negative, updated on the viewport's resize and scroll without React state, removed on unmount. `EDITOR_TOAST_VIEWPORT_CLASS`'s bottom is `max(var(--editor-bottom-inset), var(--keyboard-inset, 0px)) + 1rem`. Chrome on Android honors the viewport's `interactive-widget: resizes-content`, so its inset reads 0 and nothing counts twice.
- **Tests.** `pnpm check` green: 4,392 tests in 339 files (4,374 in 338 at `ac45949`), 18 new, lint at the baseline's five warnings in tracked files (a sixth is in `scripts/.tmp/measure-corpus.ts`, another session's untracked scratch script). The search pane's three: Shift+Enter parks the selected card with the box's quantity, never the add or the swap, and clears and refocuses the box; its refusal on the live line; the hint line's words and order, and Shift+Enter as Enter without a Maybe list. Its layout test is rewritten for the selected row (the second line at every pointer, nothing on hover) and an unselected one (hidden; touch's second line; hover's first line, third column, with the meta hidden and the leader's button off). Combos' five: no button without `onMaybe`; "Add · Maybe" parking over one resolve with no add and nothing on the live line; "Swap in… · Maybe" at the maximum; "In Maybe" for a parked piece and neither for a deck piece; a refusal on the live line. The card pane's one: Maybe after Add, 44 px on touch. The editor's four: the phone sheet's Maybe at 100 / 100 (one PUT, the sheet turned to "Swap in… · Remove from Maybe", its Undo turning it back); Combos' Maybe through the editor at 100 / 100 (the toast, "Maybe1", "In Maybe", one PUT, the Undo); the `?` sheet's row for Magic and none for One Piece; the editor writing the keyboard's inset and taking it away on unmount. The hook's five: the inset's arithmetic (offsetTop, zoom, never negative), resize and scroll, unmount, no `visualViewport`, and the toast class. Eighteen one-line mutations of the new lines (the key, the hint's item, the leader's button off hover, the meta stepping aside, hover's column, the selected row's actions, Combos' `inMaybe`, its deck-piece guard and park-not-add, the editor's three wirings and its hook, the sheet's button, the `?` row, the inset's offsetTop and zoom, the toast class): all caught on the first run.
- **`pnpm build`** from clean, in a detached worktree beside a build of `ac45949`: the route table is `ac45949`'s line for line (82 lines with its heading); one stylesheet is the same file (5,721 bytes) and the other is 303 bytes larger (172,039 → 172,342: the hover's placement, the leader's button off hover and the toast's bottom); client scripts are 1,892 bytes larger. No server code changed, so the smokes were not run.

In search, "2 goblin guide" and Shift+Enter: "Added 2× Goblin Guide to Maybe", one save, "Maybe3", the box empty and focused. - **Amended after the ship** (2026-11-26, `63781ab`). A read-only cross-check of this section against the code ran after the push: six checkers over 200 claims, each doubted claim put to a refuter; three findings held and one was refuted. The one that mattered: hover gave the buttons the row's whole meta cell, so a parked card's "In Maybe" and a deck card's ×N vanished under the pointer, where "Maybe from the search rows too" says a parked row keeps its mark. Now hover hides only the pips: a row with a mark keeps it, and its buttons take a fourth column, which the grid makes for that row alone; a row without a mark is as shipped. Read with a real pointer on the dev branch at 100 / 100 (a precon's draft, saved once a card was parked, then deleted with its own key, 204 and 404): "Lightning · In Maybe · Swap in…" (the name 109 px, the mark 50, the button 70), "Goblin Lackey · ×1 · Swap in…" (the name 146 px), and "Lazier Goblin · Swap in… · Maybe" as shipped (108 px); every row 44 px, and the rows' tops the same with the pointer on a row and off it. One new test (the marked rows keep the cell and hide the pips, their buttons in the fourth column; the selected row hides nothing), four one-line mutations of the new lines all caught, `pnpm check` green with 4,393 tests. A clean build of `63781ab` in a detached worktree: the route table is `2aae9a8`'s line for line, one stylesheet the same file and the other 166 bytes larger (172,508), client scripts 134 bytes larger. The two other findings were code comments the change had made stale, fixed in the same commit: the `?` sheet's "seven-row reference", and the callers named for `handleMaybeAdd`, `useResolvedAdd`'s `onMaybe`, `inMaybe`, `MAYBE_COPY` and the card sheet's actions. The refuted one is still worth saying: the leader's button leaves hover in every game, so a hovered One Piece row shows its main add alone where it showed "Add · Leader" (the selected row, touch and Ctrl/Cmd+Enter keep it), and One Piece's hint line takes the new order, with no Shift+Enter item. Not on production when this was written: its push waits for the owner's word.
